import pandas as pd
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons

print("Querying CCLE.csv for Classification = 'BFB' and selecting key columns...")
result = query_amplicons(classification='BFB',
                         csv_path="./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv",
                         select=['Sample name','AA amplicon number','Classification','Captured interval length'],
                         add_normalized_columns=True)
print("Saved query result to variable 'result'.")
print("Type of result:", type(result))

# Print basic summary of returned object
try:
    # If result is a pandas DataFrame or dict with dataframe
    if hasattr(result, 'shape'):
        print("Result shape:", result.shape)
        print("First 10 rows:")
        print(result.head(10))
    else:
        # If result is a dict-like
        print("Result keys:", list(result.keys())[:20])
        # Try to print first few rows if contains 'rows' or similar
        if 'rows' in result:
            df = pd.DataFrame(result['rows'])
            print("Converted 'rows' to DataFrame with shape:", df.shape)
            print(df.head(10))
            result = df
        elif 'data' in result:
            df = pd.DataFrame(result['data'])
            print("Converted 'data' to DataFrame with shape:", df.shape)
            print(df.head(10))
            result = df
        else:
            print("Unexpected result format; printing the object:")
            print(result)
except Exception as e:
    print("Error while inspecting result:", str(e))

# Ensure we have a DataFrame in 'result'
if not isinstance(result, pd.DataFrame):
    # try to coerce
    try:
        result = pd.DataFrame(result)
        print("Coerced result to DataFrame. Shape:", result.shape)
    except Exception as e:
        print("Failed to coerce result to DataFrame:", e)

print("\nExtracting 'Captured interval length' column (case-insensitive search)...")
col_candidates = [c for c in result.columns if c.lower().replace('_',' ') .strip() .startswith('captured interval')]
print("Matched columns:", col_candidates)
if len(col_candidates) == 0:
    # try other normalization
    col_candidates = [c for c in result.columns if 'captured' in c.lower() and 'interval' in c.lower()]
    print("Fallback matched columns:", col_candidates)

captured_col = col_candidates[0] if col_candidates else None
print("Using column:", captured_col)

if captured_col is None:
    print("No captured interval column found. Exiting.")
else:
    # Convert to numeric, handle missing/empty strings
    captured_raw = result[captured_col].astype(str).replace({'': None, 'nan': None, 'None': None})
    # Remove any commas or non-numeric characters
    captured_clean = captured_raw.str.replace(r'[^\d.]', '', regex=True)
    captured_numeric = pd.to_numeric(captured_clean, errors='coerce')
    print("Number of BFB amplicons retrieved:", len(captured_numeric))
    print("Number of non-missing captured interval length values:", captured_numeric.count())

    # Compute summary statistics
    stats = captured_numeric.describe()
    from math import isnan
    median = captured_numeric.median()
    q1 = captured_numeric.quantile(0.25)
    q3 = captured_numeric.quantile(0.75)
    iqr = q3 - q1
    std = captured_numeric.std()
    stats_dict = {
        'count': int(stats['count']),
        'min': float(stats['min']) if not pd.isna(stats['min']) else None,
        '25%': float(q1) if not pd.isna(q1) else None,
        '50% (median)': float(median) if not pd.isna(median) else None,
        '75%': float(q3) if not pd.isna(q3) else None,
        'max': float(stats['max']) if not pd.isna(stats['max']) else None,
        'mean': float(stats['mean']) if not pd.isna(stats['mean']) else None,
        'std': float(std) if not pd.isna(std) else None,
        'IQR': float(iqr) if not pd.isna(iqr) else None
    }
    print("\nSummary statistics for Captured interval length (bp):")
    print(stats_dict)

    # Histogram with 10 bins
    hist_counts, bin_edges = pd.np.histogram(captured_numeric.dropna(), bins=10)
    hist_list = list(map(int, hist_counts))
    bin_edges_list = list(map(float, bin_edges))
    print("\nHistogram (10 bins) counts:", hist_list)
    print("Bin edges (bp):", bin_edges_list)

    # Also compute counts for human-friendly bins
    bins_human = [0, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6, 1e9]
    labels = ['<=1kb','1kb-10kb','10kb-100kb','100kb-1Mb','>1Mb']
    hist_human = pd.cut(captured_numeric.dropna(), bins=bins_human, labels=labels, right=True, include_lowest=True)
    hist_human_counts = hist_human.value_counts().reindex(labels).fillna(0).astype(int).to_dict()
    print("\nCounts in human-friendly bins:")
    print(hist_human_counts)

    # Save outputs for user display
    output = {
        'stats': stats_dict,
        'histogram_counts_10bins': hist_list,
        'histogram_bin_edges_10bins': bin_edges_list,
        'human_bins_counts': hist_human_counts
    }

    print("\nPrepared output object with summary statistics and histograms.")
    # Print a few example rows with non-missing captured lengths
    print("\nExample rows with non-missing captured interval length (first 10):")
    example_rows = result.loc[captured_numeric.dropna().index].head(10)
    print(example_rows)

# Print final note
print("\nDone.")

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import re
from collections import Counter

print("Step: Querying amplicons with classification='BFB' from CCLE.csv using correct column names")

# Query BFB amplicons; request Oncogenes and identifiers. Use CCLE.csv in the data lake.
result = query_amplicons(
    classification='BFB',
    select=['Oncogenes', 'Sample name', 'AA amplicon number', 'Classification'],
    csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv',
    add_normalized_columns=True
)

print("Saved query result to variable 'result'. Now printing a short summary / preview:")

# Inspect result structure and extract rows
rows = []
if isinstance(result, dict):
    # try common keys that the tool might use
    for k in ['rows','data','results','records']:
        if k in result and isinstance(result[k], list):
            rows = result[k]
            print(f"Found list under key '{k}', number of rows:", len(rows))
            break
    else:
        # fallback: if dict appears to be list-like mapping indexes, attempt to find top-level list values
        potential_lists = [v for v in result.values() if isinstance(v, list)]
        if potential_lists:
            rows = potential_lists[0]
            print("Took first list-like value from result dict as rows, length:", len(rows))
        else:
            # maybe result is a single record dict
            rows = [result]
            print("Result is a single record dict; treating as one row.")
elif isinstance(result, list):
    rows = result
    print("Result is a list. Number of rows:", len(rows))
else:
    try:
        rows = result.to_dict(orient='records')
        print("Converted result to records, number of rows:", len(rows))
    except Exception as e:
        print("Could not interpret result structure:", e)
        rows = []

# Save rows for downstream parsing
saved_rows = rows
print("Saved parsed rows into variable 'saved_rows'. Number of amplicons found:", len(saved_rows))

# Preview the Oncogenes field for the first 10 rows to see format
print("\nPreview of 'Oncogenes' fields for first up to 10 rows:")
for i, r in enumerate(saved_rows[:10]):
    onc_field = None
    # Try keys in original capitalization
    for k in ['Oncogenes', 'oncogenes', 'Oncogene', 'Oncogenes_normalized']:
        if isinstance(r, dict) and k in r:
            onc_field = r[k]
            key_used = k
            break
    print(f"Row {i+1} (key='{key_used}' if set):", repr(onc_field))

# If no rows found, stop
if len(saved_rows) == 0:
    print("No BFB amplicons found or unable to parse query result. Exiting this step.")
else:
    # Step 2 completed successfully
    print("\nProceeding to parse Oncogenes and count frequencies across BFB amplicons.")
    # Build counts: count presence of each oncogene per amplicon (one count per amplicon per gene)
    gene_counter = Counter()
    total_amplicons = 0
    for r in saved_rows:
        # extract Oncogenes field
        onc_field = None
        for k in ['Oncogenes', 'oncogenes', 'Oncogene']:
            if isinstance(r, dict) and k in r:
                onc_field = r[k]
                break
        # Normalize and split
        if onc_field is None:
            continue
        # Some entries may already be lists; handle that
        genes = []
        if isinstance(onc_field, list):
            genes = [str(g).strip() for g in onc_field if str(g).strip()!='']
        else:
            # split on common delimiters and non-alphanumeric characters
            # Keep hyphens (some gene symbols include hyphens) and digits
            # Use regex to split on anything that's not letter, number, hyphen, or underscore
            parts = re.split(r'[^A-Za-z0-9\-_]+', str(onc_field))
            genes = [p.strip() for p in parts if p.strip()!='']
        # Upper-case genes (gene symbols are usually uppercase) and deduplicate per amplicon
        genes_norm = set([g.upper() for g in genes])
        if len(genes_norm) > 0:
            total_amplicons += 1
            for g in genes_norm:
                gene_counter[g] += 1

    print(f"Processed {total_amplicons} BFB amplicons with non-empty Oncogenes fields.")
    # Get top 25
    top25 = gene_counter.most_common(25)
    print("\nTop 25 oncogenes by number of BFB amplicons containing them (gene: count):")
    for gene, cnt in top25:
        print(gene, ":", cnt)

# Save outputs for later use
result_variable = result
saved_rows_variable = saved_rows
top25_variable = top25 if 'top25' in locals() else []
print("\nSaved outputs into variables: result_variable, saved_rows_variable, top25_variable.")

<execute>
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import os
import numpy as np

print("Step: Re-loading CCLE.csv to ensure df is available and saving result to 'result'.")
csv_path = "./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv"
result = query_amplicons(csv_path=csv_path, limit=20000)
print("Result saved to variable 'result'. Keys:", list(result.keys()))

# Convert rows to dataframe
rows = result['rows']
df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print("DataFrame shape:", df.shape)
print("Columns of interest: 'classification' and 'tissue_of_origin' (normalized names)")
print(df[['classification','tissue_of_origin']].head(6))

# Step 3: Aggregate counts by classification and tissue_of_origin
print("\nAggregating counts by tissue_of_origin and classification...")
# Drop rows with missing classification or tissue_of_origin
agg_df = df[['classification','tissue_of_origin']].dropna(subset=['classification','tissue_of_origin']).copy()

# Standardize classification strings (strip whitespace)
agg_df['classification'] = agg_df['classification'].astype(str).str.strip()
agg_df['tissue_of_origin'] = agg_df['tissue_of_origin'].astype(str).str.strip()

counts = pd.crosstab(agg_df['tissue_of_origin'], agg_df['classification'])
counts = counts.sort_values(by=counts.sum(axis=1), ascending=False)  # sort tissues by total amplicon count
print("\nCounts (rows=tissue, columns=classification). Shape:", counts.shape)
print(counts.head(20))

# Step 4: Plot absolute counts and 100%-stacked proportions side-by-side
plot_filename = "amplicon_class_distribution_by_tissue.png"
print(f"\nCreating and saving plot to {plot_filename} ...")

# Prepare data for plotting
tissues = counts.index.tolist()
classes = counts.columns.tolist()
totals = counts.sum(axis=1)

# Colors for classes (consistent order)
color_map = {
    'ecDNA': '#1f77b4',
    'BFB': '#ff7f0e',
    'Linear': '#2ca02c',
    'Complex-non-cyclic': '#d62728'
}
colors = [color_map.get(c, None) for c in classes]

fig, axes = plt.subplots(1,2, figsize=(16,6), gridspec_kw={'width_ratios':[1.2,1]})

# Absolute counts stacked bar
ax = axes[0]
bottom = np.zeros(len(counts))
for i, c in enumerate(classes):
    vals = counts[c].values
    ax.bar(tissues, vals, bottom=bottom, label=c, color=colors[i] if colors[i] else None)
    bottom += vals
ax.set_xticks(range(len(tissues)))
ax.set_xticklabels(tissues, rotation=90, fontsize=8)
ax.set_ylabel("Amplicon count")
ax.set_title("Amplicon class counts by tissue (sorted by total count)")
ax.legend(title="Classification", bbox_to_anchor=(1.05, 1), loc='upper left')

# 100% stacked proportions
ax2 = axes[1]
prop = counts.div(counts.sum(axis=1), axis=0).fillna(0)
bottom = np.zeros(len(prop))
for i, c in enumerate(classes):
    vals = prop[c].values
    ax2.bar(tissues, vals, bottom=bottom, label=c, color=colors[i] if colors[i] else None)
    bottom += vals
ax2.set_xticks(range(len(tissues)))
ax2.set_xticklabels(tissues, rotation=90, fontsize=8)
ax2.set_ylabel("Proportion of amplicons")
ax2.set_title("Proportional distribution (100% stacked) by tissue")
# Keep legend only on first axis to avoid duplication
ax2.set_ylim(0,1)

plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig(plot_filename, dpi=150, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.close(fig)

# Step 5: Print summary and path
print("\nTop tissues by total amplicon count (top 10):")
print(counts.sum(axis=1).sort_values(ascending=False).head(10))

print(f"\nPlot saved to: {os.path.abspath(plot_filename)}")
print("\nReturning counts table (first 30 tissues shown):")
print(counts.head(30))

# Save counts table to CSV for user's convenience
counts_csv = "amplicon_class_counts_by_tissue.csv"
counts.to_csv(counts_csv)
print(f"\nCounts table saved to: {os.path.abspath(counts_csv)}")

# Save outputs into a small dictionary for clarity if needed
outputs = {
    "plot_file": os.path.abspath(plot_filename),
    "counts_csv": os.path.abspath(counts_csv),
    "counts_head": counts.head(30)
}
print("\nOutputs dictionary prepared and printed below:")
print(outputs)

import os
import pandas as pd
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import numpy as np

# File names from prior run
plot_filename = "amplicon_class_distribution_by_tissue.png"
counts_csv = "amplicon_class_counts_by_tissue.csv"

print("Checking for outputs...")

plot_exists = os.path.exists(plot_filename)
csv_exists = os.path.exists(counts_csv)

print(f"Plot file exists: {plot_exists} -> {os.path.abspath(plot_filename) if plot_exists else 'MISSING'}")
print(f"Counts CSV exists: {csv_exists} -> {os.path.abspath(counts_csv) if csv_exists else 'MISSING'}")

# If CSV exists, load it and show top rows
if csv_exists:
    counts = pd.read_csv(counts_csv, index_col=0)
    print("\nCounts CSV loaded. Shape:", counts.shape)
    print("\nTop 30 rows of counts table (tissue x classification):")
    print(counts.head(30))
else:
    print("\nCounts CSV missing. Will re-generate counts by re-querying the data.")
    from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
    result = query_amplicons(csv_path="./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv", limit=20000)
    rows = result['rows']
    df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
    agg_df = df[['classification','tissue_of_origin']].dropna(subset=['classification','tissue_of_origin']).copy()
    agg_df['classification'] = agg_df['classification'].astype(str).str.strip()
    agg_df['tissue_of_origin'] = agg_df['tissue_of_origin'].astype(str).str.strip()
    counts = pd.crosstab(agg_df['tissue_of_origin'], agg_df['classification'])
    counts = counts.sort_values(by=counts.sum(axis=1), ascending=False)
    counts.to_csv(counts_csv)
    print("Counts regenerated and saved to", os.path.abspath(counts_csv))
    print(counts.head(30))

    print("\nPlot missing; creating plot now...")
    tissues = counts.index.tolist()
    classes = counts.columns.tolist()
    color_map = {
        'ecDNA': '#1f77b4',
        'BFB': '#ff7f0e',
        'Linear': '#2ca02c',
        'Complex-non-cyclic': '#d62728'

    colors = [color_map.get(c, None) for c in classes]

    fig, axes = plt.subplots(1,2, figsize=(16,6), gridspec_kw={'width_ratios':[1.2,1]})

    # Absolute counts stacked bar

<execute>
import pandas as pd
import os

# Load the counts CSV generated previously
counts_csv = "amplicon_class_counts_by_tissue.csv"
plot_file = "amplicon_class_distribution_by_tissue.png"

print("Loading counts CSV:", counts_csv)
counts = pd.read_csv(counts_csv, index_col=0)

# Compute totals and helpful summaries
num_tissues = counts.shape[0]
classes = counts.columns.tolist()
per_class_totals = counts.sum(axis=0).to_dict()
per_tissue_totals = counts.sum(axis=1).sort_values(ascending=False)
grand_total = counts.values.sum()

print("\nSummary:")
print(f"- Counts CSV path: {os.path.abspath(counts_csv)}")
print(f"- Plot path: {os.path.abspath(plot_file)}")
print(f"- Number of tissues with at least one classified amplicon: {num_tissues}")
print(f"- Total amplicons counted (non-missing classification & tissue): {grand_total}")
print("\nPer-class totals (across all tissues):")
for c, v in per_class_totals.items():
    print(f"  {c}: {v}")

print("\nTop 10 tissues by total amplicon count:")
print(per_tissue_totals.head(10))

print("\nFull counts table (tissue x classification) - showing top 30 tissues (if present):")
print(counts.head(30))

# Save a short summary CSV as well for convenience
summary_csv = "amplicon_class_counts_summary.csv"
summary_df = counts.copy()
summary_df['total_by_tissue'] = summary_df.sum(axis=1)
summary_df.to_csv(summary_csv)
print(f"\nSaved counts summary with totals to: {os.path.abspath(summary_csv)}")

# Prepare outputs dict and print
outputs = {
    "counts_csv": os.path.abspath(counts_csv),
    "plot_file": os.path.abspath(plot_file),
    "summary_csv": os.path.abspath(summary_csv),
    "num_tissues": num_tissues,
    "grand_total": int(grand_total),
    "per_class_totals": per_class_totals
}
print("\nOutputs ready:")
print(outputs)

outputs

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd

print("Step 1: Querying amplicon table (CCLE.csv) for relevant columns...")

# Query the full CCLE amplicon table and request normalized columns to make downstream handling easier.
# We request a reasonably small set of columns by using select if available; otherwise fetch defaults.
result_all = query_amplicons(csv_path="./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv", add_normalized_columns=True)

# Save and print the raw result object so the system records the call
print("Saved result to variable 'result_all'. Printing a summary of the returned object:")
print(type(result_all))
# Try to display the first rows depending on returned type
df = None
if isinstance(result_all, pd.DataFrame):
    df = result_all
    print("Result is a DataFrame. Rows:", len(df))
    print(df.head(10))
elif isinstance(result_all, dict):
    # Commonly the tool returns {'rows': DataFrame or list} or {'data': ...}
    for k in ["rows", "data", "results", "df"]:
        if k in result_all:
            candidate = result_all[k]
            if isinstance(candidate, pd.DataFrame):
                df = candidate
                break
            elif isinstance(candidate, list):
                try:
                    df = pd.DataFrame(candidate)
                    break
                except Exception:
                    pass
    # If still no df, try converting the whole dict to DataFrame if it looks tabular
    if df is None:
        try:
            df = pd.DataFrame(result_all)
        except Exception:
            pass
    if df is not None:
        print("Extracted DataFrame from result dict. Rows:", len(df))
        print(df.head(10))
    else:
        print("Could not auto-extract a DataFrame from result_all. Printed keys:")
        print(list(result_all.keys()))
else:
    print("Unexpected return type that is not DataFrame or dict. Attempting to convert to DataFrame...")
    try:
        df = pd.DataFrame(result_all)
        print("Converted to DataFrame. Rows:", len(df))
        print(df.head(10))
    except Exception as e:
        print("Conversion failed:", e)

# If we don't have a usable DataFrame, raise an informative error
if df is None:
    raise RuntimeError("Unable to extract tabular data from query_amplicons output. Aborting.")

print("\nStep 2: Normalizing column names to expected snake_case names (if present).")
# Normalize column names to lower snake_case to find sample_name, tissue_of_origin, classification
cols_lower = {c: c.strip() for c in df.columns}
df.columns = [c.strip() for c in df.columns]

# Try common normalized column names
possible_sample_cols = ["sample_name", "Sample Name", "sample", "Sample"]
possible_tissue_cols = ["tissue_of_origin", "Tissue of Origin", "tissue", "tissue_of_origin_name"]
possible_class_cols = ["classification", "Classification", "class"]

def pick_column(df, candidates):
    for c in candidates:
        if c in df.columns:
            return c
    # try case-insensitive match
    lower_map = {col.lower(): col for col in df.columns}
    for c in candidates:
        if c.lower() in lower_map:
            return lower_map[c.lower()]
    return None

sample_col = pick_column(df, possible_sample_cols)
tissue_col = pick_column(df, possible_tissue_cols)
class_col = pick_column(df, possible_class_cols)

print("Detected columns - sample_col:", sample_col, "tissue_col:", tissue_col, "class_col:", class_col)
if not (sample_col and tissue_col and class_col):
    raise RuntimeError("Could not detect required columns (sample name, tissue, classification) in the data. Columns available: " + ", ".join(df.columns))

# Keep only relevant columns
df_small = df[[sample_col, tissue_col, class_col]].copy()
df_small.columns = ["sample_name", "tissue_of_origin", "classification"]
print("Preview of normalized subset:")
print(df_small.head(10))

# Compute per-tissue unique sample totals and number with any ecDNA
print("\nComputing per-tissue sample counts and ecDNA-positive sample counts...")

# Total unique samples per tissue
total_per_tissue = df_small.drop_duplicates(subset=["sample_name"] ).groupby("tissue_of_origin")["sample_name"].count().rename("total_samples")

# For ecDNA, find samples that have at least one row with classification == 'ecDNA' (case-insensitive)
is_ecdna = df_small["classification"].astype(str).str.lower() == "ecdna"
ecdna_samples = df_small[is_ecdna].drop_duplicates(subset=["sample_name"]).groupby("tissue_of_origin")["sample_name"].count().rename("ecdna_samples")

# Combine into one table
summary = pd.concat([total_per_tissue, ecdna_samples], axis=1).fillna(0)
summary["ecdna_samples"] = summary["ecdna_samples"].astype(int)
summary["fraction_with_ecDNA"] = summary["ecdna_samples"] / summary["total_samples"]
summary = summary.sort_values("fraction_with_ecDNA", ascending=False)

print("\nPer-tissue summary (sorted by fraction_with_ecDNA):")
print(summary.reset_index().head(200))

# Save outputs to variables and print final summary
result_summary = summary.reset_index()
print("\nSaved result to variable 'result_summary'.")
print(result_summary)

# For readability, also print top 20 tissues
print("\nTop 20 tissues by fraction_with_ecDNA:")
print(result_summary.head(20))

Do Complexity scores differ between ecDNA vs BFB vs Linear vs Complex-non-cyclic?
================================== Ai Message ==================================

Thinking and reasoning:
I will load the amplicon records, extract complexity scores and classification labels, compute summary statistics per classification, and run appropriate statistical tests to determine if complexity scores differ between classifications (ecDNA, BFB, Linear, Complex-non-cyclic). I'll use a non-parametric global test (Kruskal-Wallis) because complexity scores may not be normally distributed, and run pairwise Mann-Whitney U tests with Bonferroni correction if the global test is significant.

Plan (checklist):
1. [ ] Query amplicon data for classification and complexity score columns.
2. [ ] Inspect the returned data (head, row counts by classification) to ensure clean values.
3. [ ] Compute summary statistics (count, mean, median, std) of complexity score by classification.
4. [ ] Run a Kruskal-Wallis test to assess global differences between classifications.
5. [ ] If significant, run pairwise Mann-Whitney U tests with Bonferroni correction; report adjusted p-values.
6. [ ] Summarize and interpret results.

I'll start with step 1.

<execute>
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
from scipy import stats
import itertools
import math

print("Step: Querying amplicon data for classification and complexity score...")

# Query relevant columns from the CCLE dataset in the data lake
result = query_amplicons(csv_path="./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv",
                         select=["Classification", "Complexity Score"],
                         add_normalized_columns=True)

# Save and print out the raw result
print("Raw query_amplicons() output:")
print(type(result))
print(result.keys() if isinstance(result, dict) else "Not a dict")

# Convert result to a pandas DataFrame depending on returned structure:
if isinstance(result, dict) and 'rows' in result:
    df = pd.DataFrame(result['rows'])
elif isinstance(result, pd.DataFrame):
    df = result.copy()
else:
    # Attempt to coerce to DataFrame
    try:
        df = pd.DataFrame(result)
    except Exception as e:
        raise RuntimeError("Unable to convert query result to DataFrame: " + str(e))

print("\nDataFrame head:")
print(df.head())

# The tool also added normalized columns; prefer normalized 'classification' and 'complexity_score' if present
if 'classification' in df.columns and 'complexity_score' in df.columns:
    df = df[['classification', 'complexity_score']].copy()
else:
    # fallback to original column names if normalized not present
    # try common variants
    col_class = None
    col_comp = None
    for c in df.columns:
        if c.lower().strip() in ['classification', 'class', 'classification']:
            col_class = c
        if c.lower().replace(" ", "_") in ['complexity_score', 'complexity score', 'complexityscore']:
            col_comp = c
    if col_class is None or col_comp is None:
        raise RuntimeError("Could not find classification or complexity score columns in returned data. Columns: " + ", ".join(df.columns))
    df = df[[col_class, col_comp]].copy()
    df.columns = ['classification', 'complexity_score']

print("\nUsing columns:")
print(df.dtypes)
print(df['classification'].value_counts(dropna=False))

# Clean data: drop missing complexity scores and missing classification
initial_count = len(df)
df = df.dropna(subset=['classification', 'complexity_score'])
after_drop_count = len(df)
print(f"\nDropped {initial_count - after_drop_count} rows with missing classification or complexity_score.")

# Ensure complexity_score is numeric
df['complexity_score'] = pd.to_numeric(df['complexity_score'], errors='coerce')
df = df.dropna(subset=['complexity_score'])
print(f"Final rows for analysis: {len(df)}")

# Step 3: summary statistics per classification
grouped = df.groupby('classification')['complexity_score']
summary = grouped.agg(['count', 'mean', 'median', 'std', 'min', 'max']).reset_index()
print("\nSummary statistics by classification:")
print(summary)

# Step 4: Kruskal-Wallis test across groups
classes = df['classification'].unique()
groups = [df.loc[df['classification']==c, 'complexity_score'].values for c in classes]
print("\nClass labels found:", classes)
# Only include groups with at least 2 observations for Kruskal-Wallis
valid_groups = [(c, arr) for c, arr in zip(classes, groups) if len(arr) >= 2]
if len(valid_groups) < 2:
    raise RuntimeError("Not enough groups with >=2 observations to perform group comparisons.")
group_arrays = [arr for c, arr in valid_groups]
kw_stat, kw_p = stats.kruskal(*group_arrays)
print(f"\nKruskal-Wallis H-statistic = {kw_stat:.4f}, p-value = {kw_p:.4e}")

# Step 5: Pairwise Mann-Whitney U tests with Bonferroni correction
from itertools import combinations
pair_results = []
group_dict = {c: df.loc[df['classification']==c, 'complexity_score'].values for c in classes}
pairs = list(combinations(classes, 2))
for a, b in pairs:
    arr_a = group_dict[a]
    arr_b = group_dict[b]
    if len(arr_a) < 1 or len(arr_b) < 1:
        u_stat, p_val = (np.nan, np.nan)
    else:
        # use two-sided test; allow ties
        try:
            u_stat, p_val = stats.mannwhitneyu(arr_a, arr_b, alternative='two-sided')
        except Exception as e:
            # if errors (e.g., all ties), fallback to p=1
            u_stat, p_val = (np.nan, 1.0)
    pair_results.append({'group1': a, 'group2': b, 'u_stat': u_stat, 'p_uncorrected': p_val})

# Bonferroni correction
m = len(pair_results)
for pr in pair_results:
    if np.isnan(pr['p_uncorrected']):
        pr['p_bonf'] = np.nan
    else:
        pr['p_bonf'] = min(pr['p_uncorrected'] * m, 1.0)

pair_df = pd.DataFrame(pair_results)
print("\nPairwise Mann-Whitney U results (with Bonferroni correction):")
print(pair_df)

# Print group medians to help interpretation
medians = df.groupby('classification')['complexity_score'].median().sort_values(ascending=False)
print("\nGroup medians (sorted):")
print(medians)

# Save results object
analysis_results = {
    'summary': summary,
    'kruskal_wallis': {'statistic': float(kw_stat), 'pvalue': float(kw_p)},
    'pairwise': pair_df,
    'medians_sorted': medians
}

print("\nAnalysis complete. Saving results to 'analysis_results' variable and printing key outputs above.")
analysis_results

                         add_normalized_columns=True)

    print("Keys in result:", list(result.keys()))
    if 'rows' in result:

df = df.dropna(subset=['classification', 'complexity_score'])
after_drop_count = len(df)
print(f"\nDropped {initial_count - after_drop_count} rows with missing classification or complexity_score. Remaining: {after_drop_count}")

# Ensure complexity_score numeric
df['complexity_score'] = pd.to_numeric(df['complexity_score'], errors='coerce')
before_drop = len(df)
df = df.dropna(subset=['complexity_score'])
after_numeric = len(df)
print(f"Dropped {before_drop - after_numeric} rows due to non-numeric complexity_score. Final rows: {after_numeric}")

# Step 3: summary statistics by classification
grouped = df.groupby('classification')['complexity_score']
summary = grouped.agg(['count', 'mean', 'median', 'std', 'min', 'max']).reset_index()
print("\nSummary statistics by classification:")
print(summary.to_string(index=False))

# Step 4: Kruskal-Wallis test across groups
classes = sorted(df['classification'].unique())
print("\nClass labels found:", classes)

groups_for_kw = [df.loc[df['classification'] == c, 'complexity_score'].values for c in classes]
# Filter groups that have at least 2 observations (scipy requires at least one, but meaningful if >=2)
valid_groups = [g for g in groups_for_kw if len(g) >= 1]
if len(valid_groups) < 2:
    raise RuntimeError("Not enough groups with data to perform Kruskal-Wallis.")
kw_stat, kw_p = stats.kruskal(*groups_for_kw)
print(f"\nKruskal-Wallis H-statistic = {kw_stat:.4f}, p-value = {kw_p:.4e}")

# Step 5: pairwise Mann-Whitney U tests with Bonferroni correction
pair_results = []
pairs = list(combinations(classes, 2))
m = len(pairs)
for a, b in pairs:
    arr_a = df.loc[df['classification'] == a, 'complexity_score'].values
    arr_b = df.loc[df['classification'] == b, 'complexity_score'].values
    if len(arr_a) < 1 or len(arr_b) < 1:
        u_stat, p_val = (np.nan, np.nan)
    else:
        try:
            u_stat, p_val = stats.mannwhitneyu(arr_a, arr_b, alternative='two-sided')
        except Exception as e:
            u_stat, p_val = (np.nan, 1.0)
    p_bonf = np.nan if np.isnan(p_val) else min(p_val * m, 1.0)
    pair_results.append({'group1': a, 'group2': b, 'n1': len(arr_a), 'n2': len(arr_b),
                         'u_stat': u_stat, 'p_uncorrected': p_val, 'p_bonferroni': p_bonf})

pair_df = pd.DataFrame(pair_results)
print("\nPairwise Mann-Whitney U results (Bonferroni-corrected):")
print(pair_df.to_string(index=False))

print("\nGroup medians (for interpretation):")
print(df.groupby('classification')['complexity_score'].median().sort_values(ascending=False))

analysis_results = {
    'summary': summary,
    'kruskal_wallis': {'statistic': float(kw_stat), 'pvalue': float(kw_p)},
    'pairwise': pair_df
}

print("\nAnalysis complete. Results saved in 'analysis_results' variable and printed above.")
analysis_results
</execute>

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import seaborn as sns
from scipy import stats
import os

print("Step: Querying amplicon table for classification = ['ecDNA','BFB'] using CCLE.csv in data lake...")

# Query the amplicon table using the data lake path
csv_path = './data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv'
result = query_amplicons(classification=['ecDNA','BFB'], add_normalized_columns=True, csv_path=csv_path)

# Convert result to DataFrame robustly
if isinstance(result, dict):
    if 'rows' in result and result['rows'] is not None:
        df = pd.DataFrame(result['rows'])
    elif 'data' in result and result['data'] is not None:
        df = pd.DataFrame(result['data'])
    else:
        # Try to find any list-like entry
        found = False
        for v in result.values():
            if isinstance(v, list):
                df = pd.DataFrame(v)
                found = True
                break
        if not found:
            raise ValueError("Could not find tabular data in query result dictionary.")
else:
    # assume list-like or DataFrame
    df = pd.DataFrame(result)

print(f"Loaded data with shape: {df.shape}")
print("Columns:", df.columns.tolist()[:50])

print("\nPreview (first 5 rows):")
print(df.head().to_string())

# Identify likely columns for captured interval length (size) and feature max copy number
cols_lower = {c: c.lower() for c in df.columns}

# find candidate for size (captured interval length)
size_candidates = [c for c in df.columns if ('captur' in c.lower() and 'length' in c.lower()) or 'captured_interval' in c.lower() or 'captured' in c.lower() or 'interval_length' in c.lower()]
# find candidate for max copy number
maxcopy_candidates = [c for c in df.columns if ('max' in c.lower() and 'copy' in c.lower()) or 'feature_max' in c.lower() or 'feature_max_copy' in c.lower() or 'max_copy' in c.lower()]

print("\nDetected candidate columns for size:", size_candidates)
print("Detected candidate columns for max copy number:", maxcopy_candidates)

# Fallback common normalized names used in this tool
fallback_size = 'captured_interval_length'
fallback_maxcopy = 'feature_max_copy_number'
fallback_class = 'classification'

# Determine final column names
col_size = size_candidates[0] if size_candidates else (fallback_size if fallback_size in df.columns else None)
col_maxcopy = maxcopy_candidates[0] if maxcopy_candidates else (fallback_maxcopy if fallback_maxcopy in df.columns else None)
col_class = None
if 'classification' in df.columns:
    col_class = 'classification'
elif 'Classification' in df.columns:
    col_class = 'Classification'
elif 'classification' in (c.lower() for c in df.columns):
    # pick the column that matches classification ignoring case
    for c in df.columns:
        if c.lower() == 'classification':
            col_class = c
            break

print("\nUsing columns - size:", col_size, ", max_copy:", col_maxcopy, ", class:", col_class)

if col_size is None or col_maxcopy is None or col_class is None:
    raise ValueError("Could not identify necessary columns for size, max copy number, or classification. Columns found: " + ", ".join(df.columns))

# Prepare dataframe with selected columns
df_sub = df[[col_class, col_size, col_maxcopy]].copy()
df_sub = df_sub.rename(columns={col_class: 'classification', col_size: 'size_bp', col_maxcopy: 'max_copy'})
print("\nSubset preview:")
print(df_sub.head().to_string())

# Convert to numeric
df_sub['size_bp'] = pd.to_numeric(df_sub['size_bp'], errors='coerce')
df_sub['max_copy'] = pd.to_numeric(df_sub['max_copy'], errors='coerce')

# Drop rows with missing or non-positive values
initial_count = df_sub.shape[0]
df_sub = df_sub.dropna(subset=['size_bp', 'max_copy', 'classification'])
df_sub = df_sub[(df_sub['size_bp'] > 0) & (df_sub['max_copy'] > 0)]
clean_count = df_sub.shape[0]
print(f"\nRows before cleaning: {initial_count}, after cleaning: {clean_count}")

if clean_count == 0:
    raise ValueError("No valid rows after cleaning. Can't proceed.")

# Add log10 transforms
df_sub['log10_size'] = np.log10(df_sub['size_bp'])
df_sub['log10_maxcopy'] = np.log10(df_sub['max_copy'])

print("\nTransformed preview:")
print(df_sub[['classification','size_bp','max_copy','log10_size','log10_maxcopy']].head().to_string())

# Compute correlations
def compute_corr(x, y):
    # return Pearson and Spearman with p-values
    pearson = stats.pearsonr(x, y)
    spearman = stats.spearmanr(x, y)
    return pearson, spearman

results = {}
# overall
pearson_overall, spearman_overall = compute_corr(df_sub['log10_size'], df_sub['log10_maxcopy'])
results['overall'] = {'pearson_r': pearson_overall[0], 'pearson_p': pearson_overall[1],
                      'spearman_rho': spearman_overall.correlation if hasattr(spearman_overall, 'correlation') else spearman_overall[0],
                      'spearman_p': spearman_overall.pvalue if hasattr(spearman_overall, 'pvalue') else spearman_overall[1]}

# by classification
for cls in df_sub['classification'].unique():
    sub = df_sub[df_sub['classification'] == cls]
    if len(sub) >= 2:
        p1, s1 = compute_corr(sub['log10_size'], sub['log10_maxcopy'])
        results[cls] = {'n': len(sub),
                        'pearson_r': p1[0], 'pearson_p': p1[1],
                        'spearman_rho': s1.correlation if hasattr(s1, 'correlation') else s1[0],
                        'spearman_p': s1.pvalue if hasattr(s1, 'pvalue') else s1[1]}
    else:
        results[cls] = {'n': len(sub), 'message': 'Not enough points for correlation'}

print("\nCorrelation results (log10 space):")
for k,v in results.items():
    print(f"{k}: {v}")

# Create scatter plot (log-log)
plt.figure(figsize=(8,6))
sns.set(style="whitegrid")

# scatter of log values colored by classification
sns.scatterplot(x='log10_size', y='log10_maxcopy', hue='classification', data=df_sub, s=40, alpha=0.7)

# add regression lines per class (in log-log space)
classes = df_sub['classification'].unique()
palette = sns.color_palette(n_colors=len(classes))
for i, cls in enumerate(classes):
    sub = df_sub[df_sub['classification']==cls]
    if len(sub) >= 2:
        sns.regplot(x='log10_size', y='log10_maxcopy', data=sub, scatter=False, label=f"{cls} fit", color=palette[i], lowess=False, ci=None)

plt.xlabel('log10(captured interval length, bp)')
plt.ylabel('log10(feature max copy number)')
plt.title('ecDNA vs BFB: size vs max copy (log-log)')
plt.legend()

out_png = 'ecdna_bfb_size_vs_maxcopy_loglog.png'

Thinking / reasoning

You asked: "For ecDNA vs BFB, how does size relate to max copy number? (scatter + correlation)". I previously queried the CCLE amplicon table, prepared the data, computed log10 correlations, and saved scatter plots and a cleaned table. - Loads CCLE.csv filtered for ecDNA and BFB,
- Cleans and log-transforms size and max copy number,
- Computes Pearson and Spearman correlations (log10 space) overall and per class,
- Saves scatter plots (log-log with regression) and the cleaned table,
- Prints the numeric results and saved filenames.

2. [ ] Inspect and select columns for captured interval length (size) and feature max copy number
3. [ ] Clean data, add log10 transforms, and drop invalid rows
4. [ ] Compute Pearson & Spearman correlations (log10 size vs log10 max copy) overall and by class
5. [ ] Create and save scatter plots (log-log) and save cleaned table
6. [ ] Print concise numeric results and file locations

I'll run the analysis now and print concise outputs. 

<execute>
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
from scipy import stats
import seaborn as sns
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import os

# Step 1-3: Query and prepare data
csv_path = './data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv'
res = query_amplicons(classification=['ecDNA','BFB'], add_normalized_columns=True, csv_path=csv_path)

# Convert to DataFrame
if isinstance(res, dict) and 'rows' in res and res['rows'] is not None:
    df = pd.DataFrame(res['rows'])
elif isinstance(res, dict) and 'data' in res and res['data'] is not None:
    df = pd.DataFrame(res['data'])
else:
    df = pd.DataFrame(res)

# Identify columns
col_class = None
for c in df.columns:
    if c.lower() == 'classification':
        col_class = c
        break
# size and max copy candidates
col_size = None
col_maxcopy = None
for c in df.columns:
    lc = c.lower()
    if 'captured' in lc and 'length' in lc:
        col_size = c
    if 'feature' in lc and 'maximum' in lc and 'copy' in lc:
        col_maxcopy = c
# fallback normalized names
if col_size is None and 'captured_interval_length' in df.columns:
    col_size = 'captured_interval_length'
if col_maxcopy is None and 'feature_maximum_copy_number' in df.columns:
    col_maxcopy = 'feature_maximum_copy_number'

if col_class is None or col_size is None or col_maxcopy is None:
    raise ValueError("Required columns not found. Found columns: " + ", ".join(df.columns))

df_sub = df[[col_class, col_size, col_maxcopy]].copy()
df_sub.columns = ['classification', 'size_bp', 'max_copy']

# numeric conversion and cleaning
df_sub['size_bp'] = pd.to_numeric(df_sub['size_bp'], errors='coerce')
df_sub['max_copy'] = pd.to_numeric(df_sub['max_copy'], errors='coerce')
df_sub = df_sub.dropna(subset=['classification','size_bp','max_copy'])
df_sub = df_sub[(df_sub['size_bp']>0) & (df_sub['max_copy']>0)].reset_index(drop=True)

# add logs
df_sub['log10_size'] = np.log10(df_sub['size_bp'])
df_sub['log10_maxcopy'] = np.log10(df_sub['max_copy'])

# Step 4: compute correlations
def corr_stats(x, y):
    pearson_r, pearson_p = stats.pearsonr(x, y)
    spearman_rho, spearman_p = stats.spearmanr(x, y)
    return {'pearson_r': pearson_r, 'pearson_p': pearson_p, 'spearman_rho': spearman_rho, 'spearman_p': spearman_p}

results = {}
results['overall'] = corr_stats(df_sub['log10_size'], df_sub['log10_maxcopy'])
counts = df_sub['classification'].value_counts().to_dict()
for cls in df_sub['classification'].unique():
    sub = df_sub[df_sub['classification']==cls]
    if len(sub) >= 2:
        results[cls] = corr_stats(sub['log10_size'], sub['log10_maxcopy'])
        results[cls]['n'] = len(sub)
    else:
        results[cls] = {'n': len(sub), 'message': 'not enough points'}

# Step 5: create and save plots and cleaned table
sns.set(style="whitegrid")
plt.figure(figsize=(7,6))
sns.scatterplot(data=df_sub, x='log10_size', y='log10_maxcopy', hue='classification', alpha=0.7, s=40)
# regression per class
for cls in df_sub['classification'].unique():
    sub = df_sub[df_sub['classification']==cls]
    if len(sub) >= 2:
        sns.regplot(x='log10_size', y='log10_maxcopy', data=sub, scatter=False, ci=None, label=f"{cls} fit")
plt.xlabel('log10(captured interval length, bp)')
plt.ylabel('log10(feature max copy number)')
plt.title('ecDNA vs BFB: log10(size) vs log10(max copy)')
plt.legend()
plt.tight_layout()
plot1 = 'ecdna_bfb_loglog_scatter.png'
plt.savefig(plot1, dpi=200)
plt.close()

# alternative with log axes (raw values)
plt.figure(figsize=(7,6))
sns.scatterplot(data=df_sub, x='size_bp', y='max_copy', hue='classification', alpha=0.7, s=40)
plt.xscale('log')
plt.yscale('log')
plt.xlabel('captured interval length (bp) [log scale]')
plt.ylabel('feature max copy number [log scale]')
plt.title('ecDNA vs BFB: size vs max copy (log axes)')
plt.tight_layout()
plot2 = 'ecdna_bfb_raw_logaxes.png'
plt.savefig(plot2, dpi=200)
plt.close()

out_csv = 'ecdna_bfb_cleaned_table.csv'
df_sub.to_csv(out_csv, index=False)

# Step 6: print concise numeric results and file locations
print("Counts by classification:")
for k,v in counts.items():
    print(f"  {k}: {v} amplicons")

print("\nCorrelation results (log10 size vs log10 max copy):")
for k, v in results.items():
    if 'message' in v:
        print(f"  {k}: n={v['n']} - {v['message']}")
    else:
        n = v.get('n', df_sub.shape[0] if k=='overall' else 'NA')
        print(f"  {k}: n={n}, Pearson r = {v['pearson_r']:.3f} (p={v['pearson_p']:.3g}), Spearman rho = {v['spearman_rho']:.3f} (p={v['spearman_p']:.3g})")

print("\nSaved files:")
print("  Cleaned table:", out_csv)
print("  Plots:", plot1, ",", plot2)
print("\nCurrent directory files:", [f for f in os.listdir('.') if f.startswith('ecdna_bfb') or f.endswith('.csv')][:20])
</execute>

Data used
- Source: CCLE amplicon table (filtered to Classification = ecDNA or BFB)
- Total amplicons: 411 (ecDNA: 297, BFB: 114)
- Variables: captured interval length (size, in bp) and feature maximum copy number
- Analysis done on log10-transformed values of both variables

Correlation results (log10 size vs log10 max copy)
- Overall (n = 411)
  - Pearson r = 0.302, p = 4.2e-10
  - Spearman rho = 0.366, p = 1.65e-14

- ecDNA (n = 297)
  - Pearson r = 0.343, p = 1.22e-9
  - Spearman rho = 0.417, p = 6.71e-14

- BFB (n = 114)
  - Pearson r = 0.188, p = 0.045
  - Spearman rho = 0.241, p = 0.0099

Plots and files saved (in current working directory)
- Scatter plot, log-log (log10 axes plotted and regression lines per class): ecdna_bfb_loglog_scatter.png
- Alternative plot with raw axes but log-scaled: ecdna_bfb_raw_logaxes.png
- Cleaned table used for analysis: ecdna_bfb_cleaned_table.csv

Interpretation
- There is a statistically significant positive association between amplicon size and maximum copy number across these amplicons.
- The association is stronger for ecDNA (moderate positive correlation) than for BFB (weaker positive correlation).
- Correlations were computed on log10-transformed size and max-copy to accommodate the wide dynamic range and to linearize relationships.

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import re

print("Step: Querying CCLE for ecDNA amplicons (Oncogenes column)...")
result_ecdna = query_amplicons(classification=['ecDNA'],
                               csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv',
                               select=['Oncogenes'],
                               add_normalized_columns=True)
print("Saved result_ecdna. Preview / summary:")
print(type(result_ecdna))
print(result_ecdna.keys() if hasattr(result_ecdna, 'keys') else "No .keys()")

# Print a small summary of returned rows (structure dependent)
print("Full result_ecdna output:")
print(result_ecdna)

print("\nStep: Querying CCLE for BFB amplicons (Oncogenes column)...")
result_bfb = query_amplicons(classification=['BFB'],
                             csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv',
                             select=['Oncogenes'],
                             add_normalized_columns=True)
print("Saved result_bfb. Preview / summary:")
print(type(result_bfb))
print(result_bfb.keys() if hasattr(result_bfb, 'keys') else "No .keys()")

print(result_bfb)

# Extract oncogene strings robustly depending on returned structure.
def extract_oncogene_strings(query_result):
    """
    Accepts the query_amplicons return and tries to extract a list of oncogene strings.
    Handles common return shapes: list of dicts, dict with 'rows' key, pandas-like structures.
    """
    onc_strings = []
    if query_result is None:
        return onc_strings
    # If it's a dict with 'rows' or 'data'
    if isinstance(query_result, dict):
        # Try common keys
        if 'rows' in query_result and isinstance(query_result['rows'], list):
            for row in query_result['rows']:
                # row might be dict with 'Oncogenes' or 'oncogenes'
                val = row.get('Oncogenes') or row.get('oncogenes') or row.get('Oncogenes ')
                if val:
                    onc_strings.append(val)
        elif 'data' in query_result and isinstance(query_result['data'], list):
            for row in query_result['data']:
                if isinstance(row, dict):
                    val = row.get('Oncogenes') or row.get('oncogenes')
                    if val:
                        onc_strings.append(val)
        else:
            # If dict maps column->list
            if 'Oncogenes' in query_result and isinstance(query_result['Oncogenes'], list):
                onc_strings.extend([v for v in query_result['Oncogenes'] if v])
            elif 'oncogenes' in query_result and isinstance(query_result['oncogenes'], list):
                onc_strings.extend([v for v in query_result['oncogenes'] if v])
            else:
                # fallback: iterate values and collect strings
                for v in query_result.values():
                    if isinstance(v, list):
                        for item in v:
                            if isinstance(item, str):
                                onc_strings.append(item)
    elif isinstance(query_result, list):
        for row in query_result:
            if isinstance(row, dict):
                val = row.get('Oncogenes') or row.get('oncogenes')
                if val:
                    onc_strings.append(val)
            elif isinstance(row, str):
                onc_strings.append(row)
    else:
        # last-resort: convert to string and try to find patterns
        try:
            s = str(query_result)
            # split lines
            for line in s.splitlines():
                if line.strip():
                    onc_strings.append(line.strip())
        except Exception:
            pass
    return onc_strings

ecdna_onc_strings = extract_oncogene_strings(result_ecdna)
bfb_onc_strings = extract_oncogene_strings(result_bfb)

print("\nNumber of raw oncogene string entries (ecDNA):", len(ecdna_onc_strings))
print("Sample ecDNA oncogene strings (first 10):", ecdna_onc_strings[:10])
print("\nNumber of raw oncogene string entries (BFB):", len(bfb_onc_strings))
print("Sample BFB oncogene strings (first 10):", bfb_onc_strings[:10])

# Function to parse gene lists from fields (split on common separators)
def parse_gene_field_list(field_string):
    if not field_string:
        return []
    # ensure string
    s = str(field_string)
    # Remove surrounding brackets or quotes
    s = s.strip().strip('[]')
    # Split on semicolon, comma, pipe, whitespace+slash, or '/'
    parts = re.split(r'[;,\|/]', s)
    genes = []
    for p in parts:
        p = p.strip()
        if not p:
            continue
        # Oncogenes sometimes include additional annotation in parentheses; strip those
        p = re.sub(r'\s*\(.*\)$', '', p).strip()
        genes.append(p)
    return genes

# Build sets
ecdna_genes = set()
for s in ecdna_onc_strings:
    for g in parse_gene_field_list(s):
        ecdna_genes.add(g)

bfb_genes = set()
for s in bfb_onc_strings:
    for g in parse_gene_field_list(s):
        bfb_genes.add(g)

print("\nUnique oncogenes found in ecDNA (count):", len(ecdna_genes))
print(sorted(list(ecdna_genes))[:50])  # print up to first 50 sorted as sample
print("\nUnique oncogenes found in BFB (count):", len(bfb_genes))
print(sorted(list(bfb_genes))[:50])

intersection = sorted(list(ecdna_genes.intersection(bfb_genes)))
print("\nIntersection (oncogenes present in BOTH ecDNA and BFB):", intersection)
print("Number of intersecting oncogenes:", len(intersection))

# Save outputs to variables for later inspection if needed
result = {
    'ecdna_unique_genes': sorted(list(ecdna_genes)),
    'bfb_unique_genes': sorted(list(bfb_genes)),
    'intersection': intersection
}

print("\nDone. Returning result dictionary.")
result

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import ast

print("Step: Querying CCLE for ecDNA amplicons (Oncogenes column)...")
ecdna_query = query_amplicons(classification=['ecDNA'],
                               csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv',
                               select=['Oncogenes'],
                               add_normalized_columns=True)
print("-> Retrieved ecDNA query. Rows returned:", ecdna_query.get('row_count_returned', 'unknown'))

print("\nStep: Querying CCLE for BFB amplicons (Oncogenes column)...")
bfb_query = query_amplicons(classification=['BFB'],
                             csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv',
                             select=['Oncogenes'],
                             add_normalized_columns=True)
print("-> Retrieved BFB query. Rows returned:", bfb_query.get('row_count_returned', 'unknown'))

def parse_oncogenes_field(field_val):
    """
    Robustly parse the oncogenes field which may be a string representation of a list
    like '["\\'MYC\\'", "\\'PVT1\\'"]' or "['']". Returns list of cleaned gene symbols.
    """
    if not field_val:
        return []
    s = field_val
    # If already a list
    if isinstance(s, list):
        raw_list = s
    else:
        # Try to literal_eval; if fails fallback to manual cleaning
        try:
            raw_list = ast.literal_eval(s)
        except Exception:
            # Attempt heuristic: strip outer brackets then split on comma
            ss = str(s).strip()
            if ss.startswith('[') and ss.endswith(']'):
                ss = ss[1:-1]
            parts = [p.strip() for p in ss.split(',') if p.strip()]
            raw_list = parts
    genes = []
    for item in raw_list:
        if item is None:
            continue
        t = str(item).strip()
        # remove surrounding quotes of several kinds
        if (t.startswith('"') and t.endswith('"')) or (t.startswith("'") and t.endswith("'")):
            t = t[1:-1]
        # if still wrapped in quotes (e.g., "'MYC'"), strip again
        if (t.startswith('"') and t.endswith('"')) or (t.startswith("'") and t.endswith("'")):
            t = t[1:-1]
        t = t.strip()
        if t == '' or t.lower() == 'nan':
            continue
        genes.append(t)
    return genes

# Build gene sets
ecdna_genes = set()
for row in ecdna_query.get('rows', []):
    field = row.get('oncogenes') or row.get('Oncogenes') or ''
    for g in parse_oncogenes_field(field):
        ecdna_genes.add(g)

bfb_genes = set()
for row in bfb_query.get('rows', []):
    field = row.get('oncogenes') or row.get('Oncogenes') or ''
    for g in parse_oncogenes_field(field):
        bfb_genes.add(g)

intersection = sorted(ecdna_genes.intersection(bfb_genes))

# Save results and print summary
result = {
    'ecdna_count_rows': ecdna_query.get('row_count_returned'),
    'bfb_count_rows': bfb_query.get('row_count_returned'),
    'ecdna_unique_genes_count': len(ecdna_genes),
    'bfb_unique_genes_count': len(bfb_genes),
    'intersection_count': len(intersection),
    'intersection_genes': intersection
}

print("\nSummary:")
print("ecDNA rows:", result['ecdna_count_rows'], "unique oncogenes:", result['ecdna_unique_genes_count'])
print("BFB rows:", result['bfb_count_rows'], "unique oncogenes:", result['bfb_unique_genes_count'])
print("Intersecting oncogenes (count):", result['intersection_count'])
print("Intersecting oncogenes list:")
print(result['intersection_genes'])

# Print first 50 of each set as examples
print("\nExamples (first 50) ecDNA oncogenes:", sorted(list(ecdna_genes))[:50])
print("\nExamples (first 50) BFB oncogenes:", sorted(list(bfb_genes))[:50])

# Save output to variable for potential later use
print("\nDone. Saved 'result' dictionary.")
result
</execute>

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd

print("Step: Querying amplicon records with classification ecDNA or BFB from CCLE.csv")
result = query_amplicons(classification=['ecDNA','BFB'],
                         csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv',
                         add_normalized_columns=True)

# Save and print the raw result
print("\nSaved query result to variable 'result'. Printing summary of 'result' ...")
print("Type of result:", type(result))

# If result is a dict with 'rows', use that; otherwise try to convert directly to DataFrame
if isinstance(result, dict) and 'rows' in result:
    df = pd.DataFrame(result['rows'])
    print("Result contained 'rows' key -> constructed DataFrame from result['rows'].")
else:
    try:
        df = pd.DataFrame(result)
        print("Constructed DataFrame from result directly.")
    except Exception as e:
        print("Failed to construct DataFrame from result:", e)
        df = None

if df is None:
    raise RuntimeError("Could not create DataFrame from query result; aborting.")

print("\nDataFrame shape:", df.shape)
print("DataFrame columns:", df.columns.tolist())

# Show first few rows for inspection
print("\nDataFrame head:")
print(df.head())

# Normalize column names for safety
cols_lower = {c: c.lower() for c in df.columns}
df = df.rename(columns=cols_lower)
print("\nNormalized columns (lowercased):", df.columns.tolist())

# Check presence of key columns
if 'sample_name' not in df.columns or 'classification' not in df.columns:
    print("Warning: expected columns 'sample_name' and 'classification' not both present. Available columns:", df.columns.tolist())

# Group by sample_name and collect unique classifications
grouped = df.groupby('sample_name')['classification'].apply(lambda s: set([str(x).strip() for x in s]))
print("\nSample -> classifications (example up to 20):")
print(grouped.head(20))

# Find samples that contain both ecDNA and BFB
target = set(['ecDNA','BFB'])
samples_with_both = [s for s,cls in grouped.items() if target.issubset(cls)]

print("\nNumber of samples containing both ecDNA and BFB:", len(samples_with_both))
print("Samples containing both ecDNA and BFB:")
print(samples_with_both)

# Save outputs for reporting
result_df = df
samples_both = samples_with_both

# Print final short summary
print("\nFinal summary:")
print("Total rows returned:", len(result_df))
print("Unique samples in result:", result_df['sample_name'].nunique())
print("Samples with both classifications:", len(samples_both))

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd

print("Step: Querying CCLE.csv for rows with classification ecDNA or BFB")
result = query_amplicons(classification=['ecDNA','BFB'],
                         csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv',
                         add_normalized_columns=True)

df = pd.DataFrame(result['rows'])
print("Constructed DataFrame; shape:", df.shape)

# Ensure we use the normalized sample_name and classification columns if present
if 'sample_name' in df.columns:
    sample_col = 'sample_name'
else:
    sample_col = 'Sample name'

if 'classification' in df.columns:
    class_col = 'classification'
else:
    class_col = 'Classification'

print("Using sample column:", sample_col, "and classification column:", class_col)

# Group sample -> set of classifications
grouped = df.groupby(sample_col)[class_col].apply(lambda s: set([str(x).strip() for x in s if pd.notna(x)]))

# Find samples that contain both ecDNA and BFB
target = {'ecDNA', 'BFB'}
samples_with_both = [s for s, cls in grouped.items() if target.issubset(cls)]

print("\nNumber of samples containing both ecDNA and BFB:", len(samples_with_both))
print("Samples containing both ecDNA and BFB (list):")
print(samples_with_both)

# For transparency, show a few example rows for the first sample if any
if samples_with_both:
    first = samples_with_both[0]
    print(f"\nExample rows for sample '{first}':")
    display_df = df[df[sample_col] == first].loc[:, [sample_col, class_col, 'AA amplicon number' if 'AA amplicon number' in df.columns else 'aa_amplicon_number', 'oncogenes' if 'oncogenes' in df.columns else 'Oncogenes']]
    print(display_df.to_string(index=False))
else:
    print("\nNo samples contain both classifications in this dataset.")

# Save outputs for reporting
samples_both = samples_with_both
result_df = df

# Print summary
print("\nSummary:")
print("Total rows returned:", len(result_df))
print("Unique samples in result:", result_df[sample_col].nunique())
print("Samples with both classifications:", len(samples_both))

I'll now run steps 2–4: inspect columns, compute summaries, and run statistical tests. I will save outputs and print them as a concise research log. <execute>from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
from scipy import stats

print("Step: Querying CCLE amplicons for ecDNA and BFB (saving result)...")
amp_result = query_amplicons(classification=['ecDNA','BFB'],
                             csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv',
                             add_normalized_columns=True)
print("Query returned rows:", amp_result.get('row_count_returned', 'unknown'))

# Save and print a preview
rows = amp_result['rows']
df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print("\nStep 2: Inspecting columns and confirming captured interval length field...")
print("Columns:", list(df.columns)[:40])
# Use normalized column name if present
if 'captured_interval_length' in df.columns:
    size_col = 'captured_interval_length'
elif 'Captured interval length' in df.columns:
    size_col = 'Captured interval length'
else:
    raise ValueError("Captured interval length column not found")

print("Using size column:", size_col)

# Keep only rows where classification is exactly 'ecDNA' or 'BFB' (case as returned)
df_filtered = df[df['classification'].isin(['ecDNA','BFB'])].copy()
print("Filtered rows (ecDNA or BFB):", len(df_filtered))

# Convert size column to numeric and drop missing/NaN
df_filtered[size_col] = pd.to_numeric(df_filtered[size_col], errors='coerce')
before_drop = len(df_filtered)
df_filtered = df_filtered.dropna(subset=[size_col])
after_drop = len(df_filtered)
print(f"Dropped {before_drop - after_drop} rows with missing size. Remaining: {after_drop}")

# Compute summary statistics by classification
def summary_stats(series):
    return {
        'n': int(series.count()),
        'mean': float(series.mean()),
        'median': float(series.median()),
        'std': float(series.std(ddof=1)),
        'iqr': float(np.percentile(series,75) - np.percentile(series,25)),
        'min': float(series.min()),
        'max': float(series.max())
    }

grouped = {}
for cls, g in df_filtered.groupby('classification'):
    grouped[cls] = summary_stats(g[size_col])

print("\nStep 3: Summary statistics by classification (captured interval length in base pairs):")
print(grouped)

# Save arrays for statistical tests
ecdna_sizes = df_filtered.loc[df_filtered['classification']=='ecDNA', size_col].values
bfb_sizes = df_filtered.loc[df_filtered['classification']=='BFB', size_col].values

print(f"\nSample sizes: ecDNA n={len(ecdna_sizes)}, BFB n={len(bfb_sizes)}")

# Check distributions roughly (Shapiro for small groups; otherwise skip heavy normality assumptions)
shapiro_ec = stats.shapiro(ecdna_sizes) if len(ecdna_sizes) <= 5000 else None
shapiro_bfb = stats.shapiro(bfb_sizes) if len(bfb_sizes) <= 5000 else None
print("\nNormality checks (Shapiro-Wilk, if computed):")
print("ecDNA Shapiro:", shapiro_ec)
print("BFB Shapiro:", shapiro_bfb)

# Perform Mann-Whitney U test (nonparametric). Two-sided and one-sided (ecDNA > BFB)
mw_two = stats.mannwhitneyu(ecdna_sizes, bfb_sizes, alternative='two-sided')
mw_greater = stats.mannwhitneyu(ecdna_sizes, bfb_sizes, alternative='greater')

# Also perform two-sample t-test (Welch's)
t_res = stats.ttest_ind(ecdna_sizes, bfb_sizes, equal_var=False, nan_policy='omit')

# Compute mean difference and Cohen's d (approx)
mean_diff = np.mean(ecdna_sizes) - np.mean(bfb_sizes)
# pooled sd for Cohen's d (use pooled from two groups)
sd_pooled = np.sqrt(((np.nanstd(ecdna_sizes, ddof=1)**2)*(len(ecdna_sizes)-1) + (np.nanstd(bfb_sizes, ddof=1)**2)*(len(bfb_sizes)-1)) / (len(ecdna_sizes)+len(bfb_sizes)-2))
cohens_d = mean_diff / sd_pooled if sd_pooled > 0 else np.nan

print("\nStep 4: Statistical tests comparing ecDNA vs BFB captured interval lengths:")
print("Mann-Whitney U (two-sided): statistic=%.3f, p=%.3e" % (mw_two.statistic, mw_two.pvalue))
print("Mann-Whitney U (alternative ecDNA > BFB): statistic=%.3f, p=%.3e" % (mw_greater.statistic, mw_greater.pvalue))
print("Welch t-test: t=%.3f, p=%.3e, df~%.1f" % (t_res.statistic, t_res.pvalue, (len(ecdna_sizes)+len(bfb_sizes)-2)))
print("Mean(ecDNA) - Mean(BFB) = %.1f bp" % mean_diff)
print("Cohen's d (approx) = %.3f" % cohens_d)

# Print a small numeric summary (medians) too
print("\nMedians: ecDNA median=%.1f bp, BFB median=%.1f bp" % (np.median(ecdna_sizes), np.median(bfb_sizes)))

# Save outputs to variables for potential later use
results = {
    'grouped_summary': grouped,
    'mw_two': {'statistic': mw_two.statistic, 'pvalue': mw_two.pvalue},
    'mw_greater': {'statistic': mw_greater.statistic, 'pvalue': mw_greater.pvalue},
    't_test': {'statistic': t_res.statistic, 'pvalue': t_res.pvalue},
    'mean_diff': mean_diff,
    'cohens_d': cohens_d,
    'n_ecdna': len(ecdna_sizes),
    'n_bfb': len(bfb_sizes)
}

print("\nSaved results to variable 'results'.\nDone.")</execute>

<solution>
Summary of analysis (CCLE.csv; captured interval length in base pairs)
- Sample sizes: ecDNA n = 297, BFB n = 114.
- Summary statistics:
  - ecDNA: mean = 2,096,639 bp; median = 985,738 bp; SD ≈ 3,299,880; min = 10,387; max = 24,593,843.
  - BFB:   mean = 2,271,737 bp; median = 1,276,367 bp; SD ≈ 2,991,727; min = 59,463; max = 21,246,834.
- Distributions: strongly non-normal (Shapiro-Wilk p << 0.001 for both groups).
- Statistical tests:
  - Mann–Whitney U (two-sided): U = 14,775; p = 0.0458.
  - Mann–Whitney U (alternative ecDNA > BFB): p = 0.977 (no evidence ecDNA > BFB).
  - Welch t-test: t = -0.516; p = 0.606.
  - Mean difference (ecDNA − BFB) = −175,098 bp (slightly smaller for ecDNA).
  - Cohen’s d ≈ −0.054 (very small effect).
- Interpretation: There is no evidence that ecDNA amplifications are larger than BFB on average in this CCLE dataset. Median and mean sizes are slightly larger for BFB. The nonparametric test is borderline significant two-sided (p≈0.046) but the direction and effect size indicate BFB tend to be slightly larger; overall the difference is very small and not robust given skewed distributions and outliers.
- Reproducibility: Analysis used ./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv; intermediate results were stored in variable 'results' during analysis.

Conclusion: No — ecDNA amplifications are not larger than BFB on average in this dataset; if anything, BFB are slightly larger but the difference is small.

# Step: Query CCLE amplicons for ecDNA with feature_maximum_copy_number > 20
print("Step: Querying CCLE amplicons for ecDNA with feature_maximum_copy_number > 20")

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons

# Perform query (save output and print it)
result = query_amplicons(
    classification='ecDNA',
    feature_max_copy_number_min=20.0001,
    csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv',
    add_normalized_columns=True
)
print("Saved query result to variable 'result' and printing a short summary:")
print({k: result.get(k) for k in ('summary','row_count_total','row_count_returned','filters_applied')})

# Convert rows to pandas DataFrame
import pandas as pd
rows = result.get('rows', [])
print(f"Number of rows returned in result['rows']: {len(rows)}")
df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print("DataFrame created. Shape:", df.shape)
print("Columns (first 40):", list(df.columns)[:40])

# Inspect normalized column names we expect
expected_cols = ['sample_name','aa_amplicon_number','classification','feature_maximum_copy_number','feature_median_copy_number','captured_interval_length']
present_expected = [c for c in expected_cols if c in df.columns]
print("Expected normalized columns present:", present_expected)

# Filter again to ensure feature_maximum_copy_number > 20 and classification == 'ecDNA' (safety check)
if 'feature_maximum_copy_number' in df.columns:
    df_filtered = df[(df['classification']=='ecDNA') & (df['feature_maximum_copy_number']>20)]
else:
    df_filtered = df.copy()

print("Filtered DataFrame shape (ecDNA & max_CN>20):", df_filtered.shape)

# Extract unique sample names
if 'sample_name' in df_filtered.columns:
    unique_samples = sorted(df_filtered['sample_name'].dropna().unique().tolist())
else:
    unique_samples = []

print("Number of unique samples with high-copy (>20) ecDNA:", len(unique_samples))

# Create a concise table with sample, amplicon number, and max CN
display_cols = []
for col in ['sample_name','aa_amplicon_number','feature_maximum_copy_number','feature_median_copy_number','oncogenes','tissue_of_origin']:
    if col in df_filtered.columns:
        display_cols.append(col)

if display_cols:
    concise = df_filtered[display_cols].copy().sort_values(['sample_name','aa_amplicon_number']).reset_index(drop=True)
    # Limit output to first 500 rows for printing safety
    print("\nConcise table of matching amplicons (up to 500 rows):")
    print(concise.head(500).to_string(index=False))
else:
    print("No display columns found; printing first 20 rows for inspection:")
    print(df_filtered.head(20).to_string(index=False))

# Save results for potential downstream use
high_copy_samples = unique_samples
high_copy_table = concise if 'concise' in locals() else df_filtered

# Print final summary list
print("\nFinal list of samples with high-copy (>20 CN) ecDNA (total {}):".format(len(high_copy_samples)))
for s in high_copy_samples:
    print("-", s)

# Save outputs to variables so they are visible in the environment
result_saved = result
df_saved = df_filtered
print("\nSaved variables: result_saved, df_saved, high_copy_samples, high_copy_table")

# Step: Query CCLE amplicons for ecDNA with feature_maximum_copy_number > 20
print("Step: Querying CCLE amplicons for ecDNA with feature_maximum_copy_number > 20")

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd

# 1) Run query
result = query_amplicons(
    classification='ecDNA',
    feature_max_copy_number_min=20.0001,
    csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv',
    add_normalized_columns=True
)
print("Query summary:", result.get('summary'))
print("Rows returned:", result.get('row_count_returned'))

# 2) Convert rows to DataFrame and inspect
rows = result.get('rows', [])
df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print("DataFrame shape:", df.shape)
print("Columns available (normalized subset):", [c for c in ['sample_name','aa_amplicon_number','classification','feature_maximum_copy_number','feature_median_copy_number','oncogenes','tissue_of_origin'] if c in df.columns])

# 3) Filter & extract unique sample names
df_filtered = df[(df['classification']=='ecDNA') & (df['feature_maximum_copy_number']>20)].copy()
unique_samples = sorted(df_filtered['sample_name'].dropna().unique().tolist())
print("Number of unique samples with high-copy (>20 CN) ecDNA:", len(unique_samples))

# Print the list of samples (one per line)
print("\nSamples with high-copy (>20 CN) ecDNA:")
for s in unique_samples:
    print(s)

# Save outputs for later use
high_copy_samples = unique_samples
high_copy_table = df_filtered[['sample_name','aa_amplicon_number','feature_maximum_copy_number','feature_median_copy_number','oncogenes','tissue_of_origin']].copy()

# Show first 50 rows of the table as a quick check (if many rows)
print("\nPreview of matching amplicons (first 50 rows):")
print(high_copy_table.sort_values(['sample_name','aa_amplicon_number']).head(50).to_string(index=False))

# Indicate saved variables
print("\nSaved variables: result, df, df_filtered, high_copy_samples, high_copy_table")

I will output the sample name, AA amplicon number, classification, captured interval length (bp), location, oncogenes, feature maximum/median copy numbers for each largest amplicon.

Current checklist:
1. [✗] Import query_amplicons and load CCLE.csv (failed earlier due to handling the returned type)
2. [✓] Inspect a small number of rows to determine available columns / normalized column names
3. [ ] Load full table into a pandas DataFrame and compute largest amplicon per classification
4. [ ] Present results with relevant fields

I'll now load the full dataset and compute the largest amplicon per classification.

<execute>
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np

print("Step: Query full CCLE dataset with normalized columns...")
result = query_amplicons(csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv', add_normalized_columns=True)

# Save and print result type
print("Type of result:", type(result))

# Extract rows robustly
if isinstance(result, dict) and 'rows' in result:
    rows = result['rows']
elif isinstance(result, list):
    rows = result
else:
    try:
        rows = list(result)
    except Exception as e:
        raise RuntimeError(f"Unable to interpret query_amplicons output: {e}")

print("Number of rows fetched:", len(rows))

# Convert to DataFrame
df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print("DataFrame shape:", df.shape)
print("DataFrame columns (first 30):", list(df.columns)[:30])

# Prefer normalized column names if present
if 'captured_interval_length' in df.columns:
    cap_col = 'captured_interval_length'
else:
    cap_col = 'Captured interval length'  # fallback

if 'classification' in df.columns:
    class_col = 'classification'
else:
    class_col = 'Classification'

# Convert captured interval length to numeric
df[cap_col] = pd.to_numeric(df[cap_col], errors='coerce')

# Filter out rows without classification or without captured length
df_filtered = df.dropna(subset=[class_col, cap_col])
print("Rows with non-null classification and captured length:", df_filtered.shape[0])

# Compute largest amplicon per classification
idx = df_filtered.groupby(class_col)[cap_col].idxmax()
largest_per_class_df = df_filtered.loc[idx].copy()

# Select columns to present
present_cols = []
for c in ['sample_name','aa_amplicon_number','classification','captured_interval_length',
          'location','oncogenes','feature_maximum_copy_number','feature_median_copy_number']:
    if c in largest_per_class_df.columns:
        present_cols.append(c)
    else:
        # try title-case fallback
        title_c = None
        for col in largest_per_class_df.columns:
            if col.lower() == c.lower().replace('_',' '):
                title_c = col
                break
        if title_c:
            present_cols.append(title_c)

print("\nSelected columns to present:", present_cols)

# Save output variable and print results
largest_per_class = largest_per_class_df[present_cols].reset_index(drop=True)
print("\nLargest amplicon per classification (rows):")
print(largest_per_class.to_string(index=False))

# Also print a neat summary per class
print("\nSummary per class (classification : captured_interval_length bp):")
for _, row in largest_per_class.iterrows():
    cls = row.get('classification') or row.get('Classification')
    cap = row.get('captured_interval_length') or row.get('Captured interval length')
    sname = row.get('sample_name') or row.get('Sample name')
    aa_num = row.get('aa_amplicon_number') or row.get('AA amplicon number')
    print(f"- {cls} : {int(cap):,} bp (sample: {sname}, amplicon #: {aa_num})")

# Save outputs for inspection
print("\nSaved results in variable 'largest_per_class' (DataFrame).")
</execute>

# Step 2: Compute median complexity per tissue and sort.
import pandas as pd

# Use the 'query_result' returned earlier (it should be in the environment)
# The rows are stored under query_result['rows'] according to prior step.
rows = query_result['rows']

print("Converting rows to pandas DataFrame...")
df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print("DataFrame shape:", df.shape)
print("Columns:", list(df.columns))

# Use normalized fields if available
if 'tissue_of_origin' in df.columns and 'complexity_score' in df.columns:
    use_tissue_col = 'tissue_of_origin'
    use_complex_col = 'complexity_score'
else:
    # fallback to original names
    use_tissue_col = 'Tissue of origin'
    use_complex_col = 'Complexity score'

print("Using tissue column:", use_tissue_col, "and complexity column:", use_complex_col)

# Clean: drop rows with missing tissue or complexity
before = df.shape[0]
df_clean = df.dropna(subset=[use_tissue_col, use_complex_col])
after = df_clean.shape[0]
print(f"Dropped {before-after} rows with missing tissue or complexity. Remaining rows: {after}")

# Ensure complexity is numeric
df_clean[use_complex_col] = pd.to_numeric(df_clean[use_complex_col], errors='coerce')
df_clean = df_clean.dropna(subset=[use_complex_col])
print("After coercion, rows with numeric complexity:", df_clean.shape[0])

# Group by tissue and compute median, count, mean (for context)
grouped = df_clean.groupby(use_tissue_col)[use_complex_col].agg(['median','count','mean','std']).reset_index()
grouped = grouped.rename(columns={'median':'median_complexity','count':'n_amplicons','mean':'mean_complexity','std':'sd_complexity'})

# Sort by median descending
grouped_sorted = grouped.sort_values('median_complexity', ascending=False).reset_index(drop=True)

# Save result variable and print top 10 tissues
result_medians = grouped_sorted
print("\nTop 10 tissues by median complexity:")
print(result_medians.head(10).to_string(index=False))

# Print total number of tissues ranked
print(f"\nTotal distinct tissues with complexity data: {result_medians.shape[0]}")

# Also print full ranked list (first 50 to avoid huge output)
print("\nFull ranked list (first 50 rows):")
print(result_medians.head(50).to_string(index=False))

# Save outputs for later steps or inspection
print("\nSaved grouped_sorted to variable 'result_medians'.")
result_medians
</execute>
