# Stepwise processing: inspect and query the CCLE amplicon file, parse oncogenes, compute exclusives.
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import ast
import re
from collections import Counter

print("Step A: Querying a small sample (limit=5) to inspect data structure...")
sample = query_amplicons(limit=5, csv_path="./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv")
print("Sample query result type:", type(sample))
print("Sample content (repr, truncated):")
print(repr(sample)[:1000])
print("\n----\n")

print("Step B: Querying full table for columns: 'Oncogenes' and 'Classification' ...")
# Request the columns by their CSV column names
full = query_amplicons(select=['Oncogenes', 'Classification', 'Sample name', 'AA amplicon number'], csv_path="./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv")
print("Full query result type:", type(full))
# Print a short summary of returned object
try:
    # If it's a pandas DataFrame-like
    df = full
    print("Rows returned:", len(df))
    print("Columns:", list(df.columns))
    print("Head:")
    print(df.head(6).to_string(index=False))
except Exception as e:
    print("Could not treat result as DataFrame directly:", e)
    # Try if it's a dict with rows
    try:
        rows = full.get('rows', None)
        print("Rows key length:", len(rows) if rows is not None else "None")
    except Exception as e2:
        print("Also couldn't access rows key:", e2)

print("\n----\n")
print("Step C: Parse oncogene entries and compute exclusivity.")

# Ensure we have a DataFrame in `df`. If `full` is dict-like with 'rows' convert to DataFrame.
if not isinstance(full, pd.DataFrame):
    if isinstance(full, dict) and 'rows' in full:
        df = pd.DataFrame(full['rows'])
    else:
        # attempt to coerce to DataFrame
        try:
            df = pd.DataFrame(full)
        except:
            raise RuntimeError("Unexpected return type from query_amplicons; cannot coerce to DataFrame.")

# Helper to parse the Oncogenes field into a list of gene symbols
def parse_oncogenes_field(x):
    if x is None:
        return []
    # If already a list
    if isinstance(x, (list, tuple)):
        return [str(i).strip() for i in x if str(i).strip()]
    s = str(x).strip()
    if s == '' or s.lower() in ("[]","['']","[\"\"]"):
        return []
    # Try ast literal eval first
    try:
        val = ast.literal_eval(s)
        if isinstance(val, (list, tuple)):
            genes = []
            for g in val:
                if g is None:
                    continue
                gs = str(g).strip()
                if gs:
                    # strip surrounding quotes if present
                    gs = gs.strip('"').strip("'")
                    if gs:
                        genes.append(gs)
            return genes
    except Exception:
        pass
    # Fallback: remove brackets and quotes then split by comma
    s2 = re.sub(r'^[\[\(]\s*', '', s)
    s2 = re.sub(r'\s*[\]\)]$', '', s2)
    # Remove repeated/embedded quotes
    s2 = s2.replace('""', '"').replace("''", "'")
    s2 = s2.replace('"', '').replace("'", "")
    parts = [p.strip() for p in s2.split(',') if p.strip()]
    return parts

# Apply parsing
df['parsed_oncogenes'] = df['Oncogenes'].apply(parse_oncogenes_field)

# Normalize classification strings
df['classification_norm'] = df['Classification'].astype(str).str.strip()

# Build sets for ecDNA and non-ecDNA classes
ecdna_mask = df['classification_norm'].str.lower() == 'ecdna'
non_ec_mask = ~ecdna_mask

# Collect gene sets
ecdna_genes = Counter()
non_ecdna_genes = Counter()

for genes in df.loc[ecdna_mask, 'parsed_oncogenes']:
    for g in genes:
        g_up = g.strip()
        if g_up:
            ecdna_genes[g_up] += 1

for genes in df.loc[non_ec_mask, 'parsed_oncogenes']:
    for g in genes:
        g_up = g.strip()
        if g_up:
            non_ecdna_genes[g_up] += 1

ecdna_set = set(ecdna_genes.keys())
non_ecdna_set = set(non_ecdna_genes.keys())

exclusive_ecdna = sorted(list(ecdna_set - non_ecdna_set))

print("Counts:")
print("Total unique oncogenes in ecDNA:", len(ecdna_set))
print("Total unique oncogenes in non-ecDNA classes:", len(non_ecdna_set))
print("Number of oncogenes exclusive to ecDNA (appear in ecDNA and never in other classes):", len(exclusive_ecdna))
print("\nTop 20 ecDNA oncogenes by frequency (gene:count):")
print(ecdna_genes.most_common(20))
print("\nTop 20 non-ecDNA oncogenes by frequency (gene:count):")
print(non_ecdna_genes.most_common(20))

print("\nList of oncogenes exclusive to ecDNA (sorted, count = {}):".format(len(exclusive_ecdna)))
print(exclusive_ecdna)

# Save outputs for clarity
result = {
    "n_ecdna_unique": len(ecdna_set),
    "n_non_ecdna_unique": len(non_ecdna_set),
    "n_exclusive_ecdna": len(exclusive_ecdna),
    "exclusive_ecdna_list": exclusive_ecdna,
    "ecdna_top20": ecdna_genes.most_common(20),
    "non_ecdna_top20": non_ecdna_genes.most_common(20)
}
print("\nCompleted processing. Result summary dict keys:", list(result.keys()))
# Print a short sample of sample rows with parsed oncogenes for verification
print("\nSample rows (first 8) with parsed oncogenes and classification:")
print(df[['Sample name','AA amplicon number','Classification','parsed_oncogenes']].head(8).to_string(index=False))

# Return result object as the execute output object
result
</execute>

from collections import Counter
import re
import math

print("Step 3: Parsing 'location' fields to extract chromosomes and count occurrences.")

# 'result' was created in an earlier step; verify it's present and has rows
try:
    rows = result.get('rows', [])
    print(f"Total amplicon rows available: {len(rows)}")
except NameError:
    raise RuntimeError("Variable 'result' not found in environment. Please run query_amplicons first.")

chr_interval_counter = Counter()
chr_amplicon_presence = Counter()
total_intervals = 0
amplicons_with_any_location = 0

chrom_re = re.compile(r'(chr[0-9XYM]+)', flags=re.IGNORECASE)

for i, row in enumerate(rows):
    # get the normalized field 'location' (lowercase) if available, else try 'Location'
    loc_field = None
    if 'location' in row:
        loc_field = row['location']
    elif 'Location' in row:
        loc_field = row['Location']
    else:
        loc_field = None

    found_chrs = set()
    if loc_field is None:
        continue

    # loc_field can be list-like or string; normalize to list of strings
    loc_items = []
    if isinstance(loc_field, list):
        loc_items = loc_field
    else:
        # attempt splitting by semicolon or comma if it's a single string with multiple intervals
        if isinstance(loc_field, str):
            loc_items = re.split(r'[;,]\s*', loc_field)
        else:
            # non-string, skip
            loc_items = [str(loc_field)]

    # iterate items and extract chromosome tokens
    for item in loc_items:
        if item is None:
            continue
        s = str(item)
        # skip empty strings or 'nan' placeholders
        if s.strip() == '' or s.lower() == 'nan':
            continue
        # search for chromosome pattern
        m = chrom_re.search(s)
        if m:
            chrom = m.group(1).lower()  # normalize to lowercase 'chr1', 'chrx', etc.
            chr_interval_counter[chrom] += 1
            found_chrs.add(chrom)
            total_intervals += 1
        else:
            # If no 'chr' found, attempt to capture leading chromosome number like '1:123-456'
            m2 = re.match(r'^\s*([0-9XYM]+)\s*[:\s]', s, flags=re.IGNORECASE)
            if m2:
                chrom = 'chr' + m2.group(1).lower()
                chr_interval_counter[chrom] += 1
                found_chrs.add(chrom)
                total_intervals += 1
            else:
                # no recognizable chromosome, skip
                continue

    if len(found_chrs) > 0:
        amplicons_with_any_location += 1
        for c in found_chrs:
            chr_amplicon_presence[c] += 1

# Prepare results: sort counters
total_amplicons = len(rows)
print(f"Total amplicons processed: {total_amplicons}")
print(f"Amplicons that have at least one recognized location interval: {amplicons_with_any_location}")
print(f"Total intervals parsed with recognized chromosomes: {total_intervals}")

# Show top chromosomes by interval count
top_by_intervals = chr_interval_counter.most_common(12)
print("\nTop chromosomes by interval occurrences (chromosome : count, percent of intervals):")
for chrom, cnt in top_by_intervals:
    pct = 100.0 * cnt / total_intervals if total_intervals>0 else 0.0
    print(f"  {chrom} : {cnt} ({pct:.1f}%)")

# Show top chromosomes by amplicon presence (at least once per amplicon)
top_by_amplicons = chr_amplicon_presence.most_common(12)
print("\nTop chromosomes by number of amplicons containing that chromosome (chromosome : amplicon_count, percent of amplicons):")
for chrom, cnt in top_by_amplicons:
    pct = 100.0 * cnt / total_amplicons if total_amplicons>0 else 0.0
    print(f"  {chrom} : {cnt} ({pct:.1f}%)")

# Save these results to variables to allow further inspection if needed
interval_counts = dict(chr_interval_counter)
amplicon_presence_counts = dict(chr_amplicon_presence)

print("\nStep 3 completed; results saved in 'interval_counts' and 'amplicon_presence_counts' variables.")

import pandas as pd
import re

print("Step: 1) Querying amplicon records from CCLE.csv using query_amplicons")

# Step 1: load all records (no filters)
result = query_amplicons(csv_path="./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv")
print("Raw result (top-level type):", type(result))

# Save and print result summary as required
print("\nSaved result to variable 'result'. Printing a short summary now:")
# Try to inspect structure
try:
    # If result is a dict-like with a 'rows' or 'data' key
    if isinstance(result, dict):
        # show keys
        print("Result keys:", list(result.keys()))
        # attempt to get a rows/data table
        if 'rows' in result and isinstance(result['rows'], (list,tuple)):
            df = pd.DataFrame(result['rows'])
            print("Constructed DataFrame from result['rows']. Shape:", df.shape)
        elif 'data' in result and isinstance(result['data'], (list,tuple)):
            df = pd.DataFrame(result['data'])
            print("Constructed DataFrame from result['data']. Shape:", df.shape)
        else:
            # fallback: try to make DataFrame directly
            df = pd.DataFrame(result)
            print("Constructed DataFrame from result dict. Shape:", df.shape)
    elif isinstance(result, pd.DataFrame):
        df = result
        print("Result is a DataFrame. Shape:", df.shape)
    else:
        # try to coerce to DataFrame
        df = pd.DataFrame(result)
        print("Coerced result to DataFrame. Shape:", df.shape)
except Exception as e:
    print("Error while interpreting result structure:", e)
    raise

# Step 2: Inspect columns to find sample identifier and oncogene columns
print("\nStep: 2) Inspecting columns to find sample and oncogene fields")
print("Columns found (first 50):", list(df.columns)[:50])

# Heuristic to find sample and oncogene column names (case-insensitive)
col_lower = {c.lower(): c for c in df.columns}
sample_col = None
oncogenes_col = None
# common sample column names
for name in ['sample', 'sample_name', 'sample.name', 'sample_id', 'sampleid']:
    if name in col_lower:
        sample_col = col_lower[name]
        break
# fallback: try columns containing 'sample'
if sample_col is None:
    for c in df.columns:
        if 'sample' in c.lower():
            sample_col = c
            break

# find oncogenes column: try 'Oncogenes' or 'oncogenes' or 'Oncogene'
for name in ['oncogenes', 'oncogene', 'oncogene(s)', 'driver_genes', 'oncogenes_list']:
    if name in col_lower:
        oncogenes_col = col_lower[name]
        break
# fallback: columns containing 'oncogen'
if oncogenes_col is None:
    for c in df.columns:
        if 'oncogen' in c.lower() or ('driver' in c.lower() and 'gene' in c.lower()):
            oncogenes_col = c
            break

print("Detected sample column:", sample_col)
print("Detected oncogenes column:", oncogenes_col)

# If we couldn't find oncogenes or sample columns, print message and stop
if sample_col is None or oncogenes_col is None:
    raise ValueError("Could not identify sample or oncogenes column automatically. Columns available: {}".format(list(df.columns)))

# Step 3: Parse oncogene lists and count unique samples per oncogene
print("\nStep: 3) Parsing oncogene lists and counting unique samples per oncogene")

# Normalize missing entries
df_subset = df[[sample_col, oncogenes_col]].copy()
df_subset = df_subset.dropna(subset=[sample_col])  # drop rows without sample id
df_subset[oncogenes_col] = df_subset[oncogenes_col].fillna("")  # replace NaN with empty string

# Build mapping gene -> set(samples)
from collections import defaultdict
gene_to_samples = defaultdict(set)

# A function to split gene lists robustly
def split_gene_list(s):
    # if empty or non-string
    if not isinstance(s, str) or s.strip()=="":
        return []
    # common delimiters: ; , | / whitespace
    parts = re.split(r'[;/\|,]+', s)
    # strip whitespace and ignore empty
    parts = [p.strip() for p in parts if p and p.strip() != ""]
    # Some entries may include annotations like "MYC (oncogene)" or gene:MYC. Remove non-alphanumeric characters except -
    clean_parts = []
    for p in parts:
        # remove parentheses content
        p2 = re.sub(r'\(.*?\)', '', p)
        # remove extra annotations after colon
        p2 = re.sub(r'.*?:', '', p2)
        p2 = p2.strip()
        # keep uppercase gene tokens (split on whitespace and take first token)
        if p2:
            token = p2.split()[0]
            clean_parts.append(token)
    return clean_parts

# iterate rows
for _, row in df_subset.iterrows():
    sample = row[sample_col]
    genes_field = row[oncogenes_col]
    genes = split_gene_list(genes_field)
    for g in genes:
        gene_to_samples[g].add(sample)

# create DataFrame of counts
gene_counts = pd.DataFrame([
    {'gene': g, 'unique_samples': len(samples), 'samples': list(samples)}
    for g, samples in gene_to_samples.items()
])
gene_counts = gene_counts.sort_values('unique_samples', ascending=False).reset_index(drop=True)

print("Total unique oncogenes found:", gene_counts.shape[0])
print("\nTop 20 recurrent oncogenes by unique sample count:")
print(gene_counts.head(20).to_string(index=False))

# Step 4: Prepare result to return to user
top20 = gene_counts.head(20).copy()

# Save results to variables for inspection
result_df = df
gene_counts_df = gene_counts

print("\nCompleted counting. Top 20 saved in variable 'top20'.")

<execute>
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import re
from collections import defaultdict

print("Step 1: Querying CCLE amplicon records from CCLE.csv")
result = query_amplicons(csv_path="./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv")
print("Saved 'result'. Type:", type(result))
print("Available top-level keys:", list(result.keys()))

print("\nStep 2: Constructing DataFrame from result['rows'] and detecting columns")
df = pd.DataFrame(result['rows'])
print("DataFrame shape:", df.shape)
print("Columns:", df.columns.tolist())

# detect sample and oncogene columns
col_lower = {c.lower(): c for c in df.columns}
sample_col = col_lower.get('sample_name') or next((c for c in df.columns if 'sample' in c.lower()), None)
oncogenes_col = col_lower.get('oncogenes') or next((c for c in df.columns if 'oncogen' in c.lower()), None)
print("Detected sample column:", sample_col)
print("Detected oncogenes column:", oncogenes_col)

if sample_col is None or oncogenes_col is None:
    raise ValueError("Could not detect sample or oncogenes column automatically.")

print("\nStep 3: Parsing oncogene lists, normalizing gene names, and counting unique samples per oncogene")

def normalize_gene_token(tok):
    if not isinstance(tok, str):
        return None
    # Strip whitespace and surrounding punctuation/quotes
    t = tok.strip().strip('\"\'` ')
    # remove parentheses content and trailing annotations like '(oncogene)'
    t = re.sub(r'\(.*?\)', '', t).strip()
    # remove prefix labels like 'gene:' or 'oncogene:'
    t = re.sub(r'^[A-Za-z]+:\s*', '', t)
    # keep only first token (in case of "MYC, other" or "MYC isoform")
    t = t.split()[0]
    # remove non-alphanumeric characters except hyphen and underscore
    t = re.sub(r'[^A-Za-z0-9\-_]', '', t)
    if t == '' or t.upper() in {'NA', 'NONE', 'N/A'}:
        return None
    return t.upper()

def split_and_normalize(field):
    if not isinstance(field, str) or field.strip() == '':
        return []
    # split on common delimiters ; , | / and also on multiple spaces
    parts = re.split(r'[;/\|,]+', field)
    tokens = []
    for p in parts:
        p = p.strip()
        if not p:
            continue
        # Some entries might have multiple gene tokens separated by whitespace - take each candidate separated by whitespace if clearly multiple all-caps tokens
        # But primarily take the first token per part
        t = normalize_gene_token(p)
        if t:
            tokens.append(t)
    return tokens

# Build mapping gene -> set(samples)
gene_to_samples = defaultdict(set)
df_subset = df[[sample_col, oncogenes_col]].copy()
df_subset = df_subset.dropna(subset=[sample_col])
df_subset[oncogenes_col] = df_subset[oncogenes_col].fillna("")

for _, row in df_subset.iterrows():
    sample = row[sample_col]
    genes = split_and_normalize(row[oncogenes_col])
    for g in genes:
        gene_to_samples[g].add(sample)

# Create DataFrame of counts
gene_counts = pd.DataFrame([
    {'gene': g, 'unique_samples': len(samples)}
    for g, samples in gene_to_samples.items()
])
gene_counts = gene_counts.sort_values('unique_samples', ascending=False).reset_index(drop=True)

print("Total distinct oncogene tokens found:", gene_counts.shape[0])

# Step 4: Report top 20
top20 = gene_counts.head(20).copy()
print("\nTop 20 oncogenes by number of unique samples (gene : unique_sample_count):")
for _, r in top20.iterrows():
    print(f"{r['gene']} : {r['unique_samples']}")

# Save outputs for inspection
result_df = df
gene_counts_df = gene_counts
top20_df = top20
</execute>

    print("Top-level keys:", list(query_output.keys()))

# Determine where rows live
if isinstance(query_output, dict) and 'rows' in query_output:
    rows = query_output['rows']
elif isinstance(query_output, dict) and isinstance(query_output.get('data', None), list):
    rows = query_output['data']
elif isinstance(query_output, dict) and isinstance(query_output.get('rows', None), list):
    rows = query_output['rows']
else:
    # The preview earlier looked like the dict itself contained the list; try to find the list-like value
    # Fallback: if the dict itself behaves like rows list (e.g., printed as a list), try to get iterable values
    # But in our earlier observation, query_output printed as dict with preview; try to find 'rows' by checking values
    found = False
    for k,v in query_output.items():
        if isinstance(v, list):
            rows = v
            print(f"Using list at key '{k}' as rows (length {len(rows)})")
            found = True
            break
    if not found:
        raise ValueError("Could not find rows list in query_output")

print("Number of raw records (rows):", len(rows))

# Convert to DataFrame
df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print("DataFrame columns:", df.columns.tolist())
print("Preview of DataFrame (first 10 rows):")
print(df[['sample_name','tissue_of_origin','oncogenes']].head(10))

# Parse the 'oncogenes' string into a cleaned list of gene symbols
def parse_oncogenes_field(s):
    if pd.isna(s):
        return []
    # s looks like a string representation of a Python list, e.g. "['']" or '["\\'E2F3\\'", "\\'SOX4\\'"]'
    try:
        parsed = ast.literal_eval(s)
        if not isinstance(parsed, (list, tuple)):
            return []
        cleaned = []
        for item in parsed:
            if item is None:
                continue
            # item may be like "'E2F3'" (with embedded single quotes)
            if isinstance(item, str):
                # strip surrounding whitespace and quotes
                itm = item.strip()
                # Remove leading/trailing single or double quotes repeatedly
                while len(itm) >= 2 and ((itm[0] == itm[-1] == "'") or (itm[0] == itm[-1] == '"')):
                    itm = itm[1:-1].strip()
                if itm != '':
                    cleaned.append(itm)
        return cleaned
    except Exception as e:
        # If literal_eval fails, try a manual cleanup
        s2 = s.strip()
        # remove outer brackets if present
        if s2.startswith('[') and s2.endswith(']'):
            s2 = s2[1:-1]
        parts = [p.strip() for p in s2.split(',') if p.strip()]
        cleaned = []
        for p in parts:
            p2 = p.strip().strip('\'" ')
            if p2:
                cleaned.append(p2)
        return cleaned

# Apply parser
df['oncogene_list'] = df['oncogenes'].apply(parse_oncogenes_field)
print("\nAfter parsing oncogene_list, preview:")
print(df[['sample_name','tissue_of_origin','oncogene_list']].head(15))

# Expand rows: one row per oncogene entry
expanded_rows = []
for _, row in df.iterrows():
    sample = row['sample_name']
    tissue = row['tissue_of_origin']
    genes = row['oncogene_list']
    if not genes:
        # Skip entries with no oncogenes
        continue
    for g in genes:
        expanded_rows.append({'sample_name': sample, 'tissue_of_origin': tissue, 'oncogene': g})

df_expanded = pd.DataFrame(expanded_rows)
print("\nExpanded rows count (after exploding oncogenes):", len(df_expanded))
print("Preview of expanded DataFrame:")
print(df_expanded.head(20))

# Deduplicate (sample, tissue, oncogene) in case same sample had multiple amplicons listing same oncogene
before_dedup = len(df_expanded)
df_expanded = df_expanded.drop_duplicates(subset=['sample_name','tissue_of_origin','oncogene'])
after_dedup = len(df_expanded)
print(f"\nDeduplicated expanded rows: before={before_dedup}, after={after_dedup}")

# Save df_expanded for next step
expanded_df = df_expanded
print("\nSaved expanded DataFrame to variable 'expanded_df' with columns:", expanded_df.columns.tolist())</execute>

<execute>import pandas as pd

# Use expanded_df from previous step (should be in the environment)
print("Columns in expanded_df:", expanded_df.columns.tolist())
# Step 4: Count unique samples per (tissue, oncogene)
group = expanded_df.groupby(['tissue_of_origin','oncogene'])['sample_name'].nunique().reset_index()
group = group.rename(columns={'sample_name':'unique_sample_count'})
print("Preview of counts (first 20 rows):")
print(group.sort_values(['tissue_of_origin','unique_sample_count'], ascending=[True,False]).head(20))

# Step 5: For each tissue, select top 5 oncogenes by unique_sample_count
top5_per_tissue = group.sort_values(['tissue_of_origin','unique_sample_count'], ascending=[True,False]).groupby('tissue_of_origin').head(5)

# For nicer presentation, assemble into a dict: tissue -> list of (oncogene, count)
top5_dict = {}
for tissue, df_t in top5_per_tissue.groupby('tissue_of_origin'):
    df_sorted = df_t.sort_values('unique_sample_count', ascending=False)
    top5_list = list(df_sorted[['oncogene','unique_sample_count']].itertuples(index=False, name=None))
    top5_dict[tissue] = top5_list

# Print results for each tissue
print("\nTop 5 amplified oncogenes per tissue (oncogene, unique_sample_count):")
for tissue in sorted(top5_dict.keys()):
    print(f"\nTissue: {tissue}")
    for gene, cnt in top5_dict[tissue]:
        print(f"  {gene}: {cnt}")

# Save results to variable for potential later use
result_top5 = top5_per_tissue
print("\nSaved final top5 table to variable 'result_top5' with shape:", result_top5.shape)</execute>

My reasoning
- To answer "Which tissues have the highest fraction of BFB features?" I need to compute, for each tissue of origin, the fraction: (number of amplicons with classification == 'BFB') / (total amplicons in that tissue). 
- I will use the provided query_amplicons function on the CCLE.csv data lake file. 
- I'll present the tissues sorted by descending BFB fraction and include counts so you can judge robustness.

<execute>
from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np

print("Step: Querying amplicon records from CCLE.csv ...")
# Query all records; add_normalized_columns so we have 'classification' and 'tissue_of_origin'
result = query_amplicons(csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv', add_normalized_columns=True)

# Per instructions: save and print the result object summary
print("Saved query result into variable 'result'.")
print("Type of 'result':", type(result))

# If result is a pandas DataFrame-like object, proceed; else try to convert
if isinstance(result, pd.DataFrame):
    df = result
else:
    # If it's a dict-like with 'rows' or similar, attempt to convert to DataFrame
    try:
        df = pd.DataFrame(result)
    except Exception as e:
        # Last attempt: if result has attribute 'rows'
        if hasattr(result, 'rows'):
            df = pd.DataFrame(result.rows)
        else:
            raise RuntimeError("Unable to convert query result to DataFrame: " + str(e))

print("DataFrame shape:", df.shape)
print("Columns available (showing up to 50):", list(df.columns)[:50])

# Show head for confirmation
print("\nFirst 5 rows:")
print(df.head(5).to_string(index=False))

# Ensure normalized column names exist
if 'classification' not in df.columns or 'tissue_of_origin' not in df.columns:
    raise RuntimeError("Expected normalized columns 'classification' and 'tissue_of_origin' not found in data.")

# Step: compute totals and BFB counts per tissue
print("\nComputing per-tissue totals and BFB counts...")
total_by_tissue = df.groupby('tissue_of_origin').size().rename('total')
bfb_by_tissue = df[df['classification'] == 'BFB'].groupby('tissue_of_origin').size().rename('bfb_count')

summary = pd.concat([total_by_tissue, bfb_by_tissue], axis=1).fillna(0)
summary['bfb_count'] = summary['bfb_count'].astype(int)
summary['bfb_fraction'] = summary['bfb_count'] / summary['total']

# Sort by fraction descending
summary_sorted = summary.sort_values(by='bfb_fraction', ascending=False)

print("\nTop tissues by BFB fraction (showing top 20):")
top20 = summary_sorted.head(20)
print(top20.to_string())

# For context, also show tissues with at least 10 amplicons (to avoid tiny-sample artifacts)
min_n = 10
robust = summary_sorted[summary_sorted['total'] >= min_n]
print(f"\nTissues with at least {min_n} amplicons, sorted by BFB fraction (top 20):")
print(robust.head(20).to_string())

# Save outputs for reference
result_summary = {
    'full_summary': summary_sorted,
    'top20': top20,
    'robust_top': robust.head(20)
}

print("\nCompleted computation. Variable 'result_summary' saved with keys:", list(result_summary.keys()))

# Print a short enumerated list of the top 10 tissues by fraction with counts for quick readability
print("\nTop 10 tissues by BFB fraction (fraction, bfb_count, total):")
for i, (tissue, row) in enumerate(top20.head(10).iterrows(), start=1):
    print(f"{i}. {tissue}: fraction={row['bfb_fraction']:.3f}, bfb_count={row['bfb_count']}, total={row['total']}")

# Expose the main dataframe and summary for further inspection if needed
df_result = df
print("\nDone.")

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np

print("Step 1: Querying CCLE amplicons selecting 'Tissue of origin' and 'Classification'...")
query_result = query_amplicons(csv_path='./data/biomni_data/data_lake/CCLE.csv',
                              select=['Tissue of origin', 'Classification'],
                              add_normalized_columns=True)

print("Saved query result into variable 'query_result'.")
print("Type of query_result:", type(query_result))

# Inspecting the returned object
if isinstance(query_result, pd.DataFrame):
    df = query_result
    print("Query returned a DataFrame. Shape:", df.shape)
    print("Columns:", list(df.columns))
    print("First 5 rows:")
    print(df.head(5).to_string(index=False))
else:
    # If dict-like or has 'rows', try to find the data container
    try:
        keys = list(query_result.keys())
        print("Query result keys:", keys)
    except Exception as e:
        print("Could not list keys of query_result:", str(e))
    
    # Common places: 'rows', 'data', or it could already be a list of dicts
    df = None
    if isinstance(query_result, dict):
        for candidate in ['rows', 'data', 'records']:
            if candidate in query_result:
                try:
                    df = pd.DataFrame(query_result[candidate])
                    print(f"Converted query_result['{candidate}'] to DataFrame. Shape:", df.shape)
                    break
                except Exception as e:
                    print(f"Failed to convert key '{candidate}' to DataFrame:", e)
    if df is None:
        # Try to convert the whole object to DataFrame if it's list-like
        try:
            df = pd.DataFrame(query_result)
            print("Converted query_result to DataFrame. Shape:", df.shape)
        except Exception as e:
            raise RuntimeError("Unable to convert query_result to DataFrame: " + str(e))

# Normalize column names (ensure we use 'tissue_of_origin' and 'classification' if present)
print("\nColumns in DataFrame:", list(df.columns)[:50])
# Identify the actual names for tissue and classification
possible_tissue_cols = [c for c in df.columns if c.lower().replace('_',' ').strip() in ('tissue of origin','tissue of origin')]
possible_class_cols = [c for c in df.columns if c.lower().replace('_',' ').strip() in ('classification','classification')]
print("Detected tissue columns candidates:", possible_tissue_cols)
print("Detected classification columns candidates:", possible_class_cols)

# Prefer normalized names if available
if 'tissue_of_origin' in df.columns:
    tissue_col = 'tissue_of_origin'
elif 'Tissue of origin' in df.columns:
    tissue_col = 'Tissue of origin'
elif possible_tissue_cols:
    tissue_col = possible_tissue_cols[0]
else:
    raise RuntimeError("Could not identify tissue column in DataFrame.")

if 'classification' in df.columns:
    class_col = 'classification'
elif 'Classification' in df.columns:
    class_col = 'Classification'
elif possible_class_cols:
    class_col = possible_class_cols[0]
else:
    raise RuntimeError("Could not identify classification column in DataFrame.")

print(f"Using tissue column: '{tissue_col}' and classification column: '{class_col}'")

# Step 3: compute totals and BFB counts per tissue
print("\nStep 3: Computing totals and BFB counts per tissue...")
total_by_tissue = df.groupby(tissue_col).size().rename('total')
bfb_by_tissue = df[df[class_col] == 'BFB'].groupby(tissue_col).size().rename('bfb_count')

summary = pd.concat([total_by_tissue, bfb_by_tissue], axis=1).fillna(0)
summary['bfb_count'] = summary['bfb_count'].astype(int)
summary['bfb_fraction'] = summary['bfb_count'] / summary['total']
summary_sorted = summary.sort_values(by='bfb_fraction', ascending=False)

print("\nTop 20 tissues by BFB fraction:")
top20 = summary_sorted.head(20)
print(top20.to_string())

# Robustness filter: tissues with at least 10 amplicons
min_n = 10
robust = summary_sorted[summary_sorted['total'] >= min_n]
print(f"\nTissues with at least {min_n} amplicons (sorted by BFB fraction), top 20:")
print(robust.head(20).to_string())

# Save outputs
result_summary = {
    'full_summary': summary_sorted,
    'top20': top20,
    'robust_top': robust.head(20)
}

print("\nSaved results in 'result_summary' dict with keys:", list(result_summary.keys()))

# Print concise top 10
print("\nConcise Top 10 by BFB fraction (fraction, bfb_count, total):")
for i, (tissue, row) in enumerate(top20.head(10).iterrows(), start=1):
    print(f"{i}. {tissue}: fraction={row['bfb_fraction']:.3f}, bfb_count={row['bfb_count']}, total={row['total']}")

print("\nCompleted all steps in plan.")

from biomni.tool.amplicon_table import query_amplicons
import pandas as pd
import re
from collections import Counter, defaultdict
from itertools import combinations

print("Step: Querying amplicons with classification = 'ecDNA' ...")
res = query_amplicons(classification='ecDNA', add_normalized_columns=True)
# Save and print the type and a brief summary
print("Returned type:", type(res))
# Try to display top-level keys and shape if DataFrame-like
try:
    # If it's a dict-like response with rows key
    if isinstance(res, dict) and 'rows' in res:
        df = pd.DataFrame(res['rows'])
    elif isinstance(res, pd.DataFrame):
        df = res
    else:
        # Try to coerce to DataFrame
        df = pd.DataFrame(res)
except Exception as e:
    print("Error constructing DataFrame from result:", e)
    raise

print("Number of rows (ecDNA amplicons):", len(df))
print("Columns available:", list(df.columns)[:50])

print("\nShowing first 5 rows:")
print(df.head(5))

# Step 2: Identify oncogene column: check likely column names
possible_cols = [c for c in df.columns if 'onc' in c.lower() or 'gene' in c.lower()]
print("\nCandidate gene-related columns:", possible_cols)

# Choose best column: prefer 'oncogenes' or 'all_genes' if present (normalized names are snake_case)
col_candidates = ['oncogenes', 'Oncogenes', 'all_genes', 'All_Genes', 'All genes', 'AllGenes']
gene_col = None
for c in col_candidates:
    if c in df.columns:
        gene_col = c
        break
# also check normalized columns
if gene_col is None:
    for c in df.columns:
        if c.lower() == 'oncogenes' or c.lower() == 'all_genes' or c.lower() == 'all genes' or c.lower().endswith('genes'):
            gene_col = c
            break

if gene_col is None:
    print("Could not automatically find a gene column. Will inspect candidate columns and pick one.")
    # fallback: list first 10 columns for manual inspection
    print(df.iloc[:, :10].head(10))
else:
    print("Chosen gene column:", gene_col)

# Step 3: Parse oncogene lists and form unordered pairs per amplicon
def parse_genes(cell):
    if pd.isna(cell):
        return []
    s = str(cell)
    # Common delimiters ; , | / whitespace -- use regex to split on semicolon, comma, pipe, slash, and whitespace
    parts = re.split(r'[;,\|/]+', s)
    # clean and drop empties and markers like 'NA'
    genes = [p.strip() for p in parts if p.strip() and p.strip().upper() not in ('NA','NONE','-')]
    # Also some entries may be single string of multiple genes separated by whitespace; split further if a space-separated list of uppercase gene symbols with no delimiters
    # but avoid splitting gene names that include spaces (rare). Keep as-is.
    return genes

if gene_col is None:
    raise RuntimeError("Gene column not found; cannot proceed without gene lists.")

# Use sample/amplicon identifiers columns if present
sample_col = None
amp_col = None
for c in df.columns:
    if c.lower() in ('sample name','sample_name','sample'):
        sample_col = c
    if c.lower() in ('aa_amplicon_number','amplicon','aa_amplicon','aa_amplicon_number'):
        amp_col = c
# fallback to first two cols
if sample_col is None:
    sample_col = df.columns[0]
if amp_col is None:
    amp_col = df.columns[1] if df.shape[1] > 1 else df.columns[0]

print("Using sample column:", sample_col, "and amplicon column:", amp_col)

pair_counter = Counter()
pair_examples = defaultdict(list)
total_amp_with_genes = 0

for idx, row in df.iterrows():
    genes = parse_genes(row[gene_col])
    # ensure unique gene symbols per amplicon
    genes = list(dict.fromkeys(genes))
    if len(genes) < 2:
        continue
    total_amp_with_genes += 1
    # generate unordered pairs
    for a,b in combinations(sorted(genes),2):
        pair = (a,b)
        pair_counter[pair] += 1
        if len(pair_examples[pair]) < 5:
            pair_examples[pair].append((row[sample_col], row[amp_col]))

print("\nTotal ecDNA amplicons with >=2 oncogenes:", total_amp_with_genes)
print("Total unique co-amplified pairs observed:", len(pair_counter))

# Step 4: Report top pairs
top_n = 20
top_pairs = pair_counter.most_common(top_n)
print("\nTop co-amplified oncogene pairs (pair : count) and up to 5 example (sample,amplicon):")
for pair, cnt in top_pairs:
    print(f"{pair} : {cnt}")
    print("  examples:", pair_examples[pair])

# Save results into a DataFrame for nicer presentation
top_df = pd.DataFrame([{'gene_a':p[0], 'gene_b':p[1], 'count':c, 'examples': pair_examples[p]} for p,c in top_pairs])
print("\nTop pairs DataFrame head:")
print(top_df.head(20))

# Save outputs to variables for further use
result_summary = {
    'num_ecdna_amplicons': len(df),
    'num_amplicons_with_>=2_genes': total_amp_with_genes,
    'top_pairs': top_pairs,
    'top_pairs_df': top_df
}

print("\nFinished analysis. Result summary keys:", list(result_summary.keys()))

# Print first 10 top pairs succinctly
print("\nTop 10 pairs summary:")
for i,(pair,cnt) in enumerate(top_pairs[:10], start=1):
    print(f"{i}. {pair[0]} -- {pair[1]} : {cnt} occurrences")

# Save result object (to satisfy "save output and print")
result = result_summary
print("\nSaved result variable 'result' containing summary.")

print("Saved output to variable 'res' and printing a brief summary of the returned object type and keys...")

# Inspect the returned structure and build a DataFrame
print("Returned type:", type(res))

    df = pd.DataFrame(res['rows'])
    print("Constructed DataFrame from res['rows'].")
elif isinstance(res, pd.DataFrame):

    # Try to coerce to DataFrame directly
    df = pd.DataFrame(res)
    print("Coerced result to DataFrame.")

# Step 3: Identify oncogene column.

print("\nCandidate gene-related columns (substring match):", possible_gene_cols)

# Prefer normalized 'oncogenes' or 'all_genes' names if present
preferred_names = ['oncogenes', 'all_genes', 'all_genes', 'oncogene', 'oncogenic_genes', 'all_genes']
gene_col = None
for p in preferred_names:
    if p in df.columns:
        gene_col = p
        break
# fallback: exact matches ignoring case
if gene_col is None:
    for c in df.columns:
        if c.lower() in ('oncogenes','all_genes','allgenes','oncogene'):
            gene_col = c
            break
# if still None, pick the best candidate from possible_gene_cols

    gene_col = possible_gene_cols[0]

if gene_col is None:
    raise RuntimeError("Could not find a gene-related column in the returned data. Columns were:\n" + ", ".join(df.columns))

print("Chosen gene column for parsing:", gene_col)

# Identify sample and amplicon identifier columns (use normalized names if available)
sample_col = None
amp_col = None
for c in df.columns:
    if c.lower() in ('sample_name','sample name','sample'):
        sample_col = c
    if c.lower() in ('aa_amplicon_number','aa_amplicon','amplicon_number','amplicon'):
        amp_col = c
# fallback to common alternatives
if sample_col is None:
    for c in df.columns:
        if 'sample' in c.lower():
            sample_col = c
            break
if amp_col is None:
    for c in df.columns:
        if 'amplic' in c.lower():
            amp_col = c
            break
# final fallback to first columns
if sample_col is None:
    sample_col = df.columns[0]
if amp_col is None:
    amp_col = df.columns[1] if df.shape[1] > 1 else df.columns[0]

print("Using sample column:", sample_col, "and amplicon column:", amp_col)

# Step 4: Parse oncogene lists and form unordered pairs per amplicon
def parse_genes(cell):
    if pd.isna(cell):
        return []
    s = str(cell)
    # remove surrounding brackets if any
    s = s.strip().strip('[]()')
    # split on semicolon, comma, pipe, slash
    parts = re.split(r'[;,\|/]+', s)
    genes = []
    for p in parts:
        # further split tokens that look like multiple uppercase gene symbols separated by whitespace
        token = p.strip()
        if not token or token.upper() in ('NA','NONE','-'):
            continue
        # Some entries may have multiple genes separated by whitespace; if so, split if all tokens look like gene symbols (uppercase letters/digits)
        space_parts = token.split()
        if len(space_parts) > 1 and all(re.fullmatch(r'[A-Za-z0-9\-\.]+', sp) for sp in space_parts):
            # treat each as gene
            for sp in space_parts:
                if sp and sp.upper() not in ('NA','NONE','-'):
                    genes.append(sp.strip())
        else:
            genes.append(token)
    # normalize genes by stripping extraneous chars
    genes = [re.sub(r'[^A-Za-z0-9\-\.]', '', g).strip() for g in genes if g.strip()]
    # drop empties and duplicates, keep order
    seen = set()
    uniq = []
    for g in genes:
        if g and g not in seen:
            seen.add(g)
            uniq.append(g)
    return uniq

pair_counter = Counter()
pair_examples = defaultdict(list)
total_amp_with_genes = 0

for idx, row in df.iterrows():
    genes = parse_genes(row[gene_col])
    if len(genes) < 2:
        continue
    total_amp_with_genes += 1
    for a,b in combinations(sorted(genes),2):
        pair = (a,b)
        pair_counter[pair] += 1
        if len(pair_examples[pair]) < 5:
            pair_examples[pair].append((row[sample_col], row[amp_col]))

    'top_pairs_df': top_df
}

        print(f"Total rows returned: {len(rows)}")
        print("First 3 rows:")
        for i, r in enumerate(rows[:3]):
            print(f"Row {i+1}: {r}")
    else:
        # Fallback: print top-level keys and sample
        print("Result type:", type(result))
        if isinstance(result, (list, tuple)):
            print(f"Total rows returned: {len(result)}")
            print("First 3 rows:")
            for i, r in enumerate(result[:3]):
                print(f"Row {i+1}: {r}")
        else:
            print(json.dumps(result, default=str)[:1000])
except Exception as e:
    print("Error printing raw result:", e)

# Update plan status after step 1 & 2 (we completed querying and inspecting)
print("\nUpdated plan status:")
print("1. [\u2713] Import and query CCLE amplicon table for Classification and Oncogenes (completed)")
print("2. [\u2713] Inspect returned result structure and save it (completed)")

# Step 3: Parse oncogene lists and compute counts
print("\nStep 3: Parsing oncogene lists and computing number of oncogenes per feature...")

# Normalize extraction of rows
if isinstance(result, dict) and 'rows' in result:
    rows = result['rows']
elif isinstance(result, list):
    rows = result
else:
    raise ValueError("Unexpected result format from query_amplicons")

# Determine column key names (case-insensitive)
# Look at the first row keys
if len(rows) == 0:
    raise ValueError("No rows returned from query_amplicons")

first_row = rows[0]
keys = list(first_row.keys())
# Find keys matching classification and oncogenes
def find_key(possible_names):
    for name in possible_names:
        for k in keys:
            if k.lower() == name.lower():
                return k
    return None

classification_key = find_key(['classification','Classification'])
oncogenes_key = find_key(['oncogenes','Oncogenes','Oncogene','Oncogene(s)','Oncogenes_list'])

if classification_key is None or oncogenes_key is None:
    raise KeyError(f"Could not find classification or oncogenes keys in data. Available keys: {keys}")

print(f"Using classification key: '{classification_key}', oncogenes key: '{oncogenes_key}'")

# Function to count oncogenes in a field
split_re = re.compile(r'[;,|/]')  # common delimiters: semicolon, comma, pipe, slash
def count_oncogenes_field(field):
    if field is None:
        return 0
    if isinstance(field, (int, float)) and (field != field):  # NaN check
        return 0
    s = str(field).strip()
    if s == "" or s.lower() in ["nan","none","[]","-","na","n/a"]:
        return 0
    # Some entries may be formatted like "['MYC', 'EGFR']" or "MYC; EGFR"
    # Remove surrounding brackets and quotes
    s_clean = re.sub(r'^\[|\]$','',s).strip()
    # Remove quotes
    s_clean = s_clean.replace("'", "").replace('"', '')
    # Split
    parts = [p.strip() for p in split_re.split(s_clean) if p.strip()!='']
    # If still a single token maybe space-separated
    if len(parts) == 1 and ' ' in parts[0] and ',' not in parts[0] and ';' not in parts[0]:
        parts = [p for p in parts[0].split() if p!='']
    # Edge: if the single token equals the empty string
    parts = [p for p in parts if p.strip()!='']
    return len(parts)

# Compute counts per classification
per_class_counts = defaultdict(list)  # classification -> list of counts
for r in rows:
    cls = r.get(classification_key)
    if cls is None:
        cls_value = 'Unknown'
    else:
        cls_value = str(cls).strip()
    onc_field = r.get(oncogenes_key)
    c = count_oncogenes_field(onc_field)
    per_class_counts[cls_value].append(c)

# Show a small sample
for cls, counts in list(per_class_counts.items())[:5]:
    print(f"Sample for class '{cls}': {counts[:5]} (total {len(counts)} features)")

print("\nStep 3 completed.")
print("3. [\u2713] Parsed oncogene lists and computed counts per feature (completed)")

# Step 4: Compute distribution (counts and percentages) per class
print("\nStep 4: Computing distribution of number of oncogenes per feature, by classification...")

distributions = {}
for cls, counts in per_class_counts.items():
    cnt = Counter(counts)  # key = number of oncogenes, value = number of features
    total = sum(cnt.values())
    # Sort keys ascending
    sorted_items = sorted(cnt.items())
    distributions[cls] = {
        'total_features': total,
        'counts': dict(sorted_items),
        'percentages': {k: (v/total*100 if total>0 else 0) for k,v in sorted_items}
    }

# Print distributions (concise)
for cls, d in distributions.items():
    print(f"\nClassification: {cls}")
    print(f"  Total features: {d['total_features']}")
    print("  Number_of_oncogenes : count  (percentage)")
    for num, count in d['counts'].items():
        perc = d['percentages'][num]
        print(f"    {num:>3} : {count:>6}  ({perc:.2f}%)")

print("\nStep 4 completed.")
print("4. [\u2713] Computed distribution (counts and percentages) per class (completed)")

# Step 5: Save final distributions to a variable and print confirmation
final_distributions = distributions
print("\nStep 5 completed.")
print("5. [\u2713] Saved final distributions (completed)")

# Print a short JSON summary of final_distributions (compact)
print("\n--- Final distributions summary (JSON) ---")
print(json.dumps(final_distributions, indent=2))

# Save outputs to variables for potential further use
query_result_saved = result
per_class_counts_saved = dict(per_class_counts)
final_distributions_saved = final_distributions

# Confirm variables saved
print("\nSaved variables: query_result_saved, per_class_counts_saved, final_distributions_saved")
</execute>
